As the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said to ‘Adī ibn Hātim:

“O ‘Adī, what makes you flee? Does the declaration ‘There is no god but Allah’ make you flee?

---

## Page 470

O ‘Adī, do you know of any god other than Allah? O ‘Adī, what makes you flee? Does the declaration ‘Allah is greater’ make you flee? Do you know of anything greater than Allah?” This demonstrates the correctness of those jurists who say that this expression may not be replaced with “Allah is great,” though a fuller explanation belongs elsewhere.

He also said:

“Glorify the name of your Lord, the Most High.”

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said: “Use this in your prostration.” Allah is therefore the Most High and the Greatest. Since knowledge corresponds to its object, knowledge and understanding of Him must be the greatest and highest of all sciences.

Second: Allah, glorified is He, is the Truth, existing through Himself. Everything other than Him is part of His creation, subject to His lordship and subdued under His power. He creates all things and brings their causes into being. Knowledge of Him is therefore the foundation and cause of knowledge of everything else, just as He Himself is their foundation and cause. Knowledge of a cause yields knowledge of the one who brings it about.

Third: To know absolute existence is to know what He has in common with everything else. This is knowledge of the middle term used in comparing Him with His creation. Clearly, this does not provide knowledge of His reality or of the reality of anything else; it provides only knowledge of an attribute shared by both. How, then, can knowledge of a shared attribute be higher than knowledge of the reality of each, together with all that distinguishes each from other kinds and individual entities?

Likewise, knowledge of absolute essence and of all such shared notions belongs to this same category.

---

## Page 471

Fourth: Absolute existence, absolute essence, and the like may refer to absoluteness in the specific sense, which excludes anything qualified—as when one speaks of “unqualified water.” What is absolute in this sense has no existence outside the intellect and mind, just as general, universal existence and general, universal essence have no external existence. This generality and absoluteness attach to realities only insofar as they are conceived in minds, not insofar as they exist as concrete entities. How, then, can the highest and noblest of sciences have mental representations, rather than actual realities, as its object? These representations merely follow those realities; otherwise, they would constitute ignorance, not knowledge.

Alternatively, these expressions may refer to absoluteness in the general sense, which excludes nothing from its scope: freedom from every qualification, even that of being absolute. Understood in this sense, the absolute does have external existence, according to the correct view. Yet it does not exist as absolute; it exists only as something particular. As for an absolute existent whose absoluteness is stipulated, it has no existence: this is the absolute in the specific sense. Since the absolute in the general sense includes what is qualified, it can indeed exist externally. Thus, if absolute existence and its concomitants do not exist externally as absolute, and nothing exists externally except as something particular, a science whose object exists only in minds, not as concrete entities, cannot be the highest of sciences.

If knowledge of mental representations could be deemed superior to knowledge of external realities, the representations themselves could be deemed superior to the realities. Knowledge of the Lord, the angels, and the prophets would then be superior to the Lord Himself, the angels, and the prophets. No rational person would say this.

---

## Page 472

Fifth:

These thinkers went wrong precisely because they based all their sciences on syllogistic reasoning. A syllogism necessarily requires a universal proposition and a middle term broader in scope than the entity described and judged—the subject, in grammatical and logical terms.

Every term and proposition has something broader in scope than itself. For example, one may speak of a human being; broader than this is animal; broader still is a growing body, then a body of the lower world, then body, then substance, and finally existent—whether existence is an essential genus, as some of them maintain, or an accidental attribute, as their more discerning thinkers maintain.

Thus, if it were said that the highest of the syllogistic sciences is the science of the existent and its concomitants, because its object is the most general of subjects, this would have some justification. Perhaps this is what they mean. Yet knowledge gained through such inference does not, by itself, yield knowledge of the reality of any existing thing unless that thing has a counterpart like it. One of the two like things is then known in itself, and the other by analogy with its counterpart. This condition cannot be met in knowledge of Allah, for He has no likeness or counterpart.

The speculative theologians, moreover, challenged them by proposing something higher than existence: the known and the mentioned. They said: “The highest category of objects of knowledge, and the most general names and terms, are ‘the known’ and ‘the mentioned,’ for these encompass both the existent and the nonexistent: the two kinds of existence—necessary and possible—and the two kinds of nonexistence—possible and impossible.”

One would therefore have to say that the highest science is that which examines the known and its concomitants. This is more general and comprehensive. A thing’s being known is something incidental to it, not an essential attribute. The same applies to its being existent, for this actually means that it is such that someone finds it. This is what the word implies:

---

## Page 473

and if some of them mean by it that a thing is real in itself, this is not its very reality—what it actually is—as has been established elsewhere. It has also been established that any philosopher or speculative theologian who says that the Lord’s reality is His existence or the necessity of His existence, or claims to have known His reality, has made a grievous error. This is comparable to saying that the reality of all other beings is their being contingent. These people are far removed from Allah, veiled from knowing Him. They have known only one universal attribute among His attributes, yet imagined that they had known His reality.

This makes it clear that when someone says that the highest science is metaphysics, which examines existence and its concomitants, it is highest only in the mind of someone seeking to know Allah by analogy with His creation. It is not highest in itself, nor is its object of knowledge the highest. Nor is it highest in the estimation of those who know the realities of existing things, or those who know Allah through their innate disposition—let alone those who know Him through revealed law, through sainthood, through revelation and prophethood, through being entrusted with a divine message, through speech, or through seeing Him.

Thus, since the ultimate attainment of the Sabian philosophers and their highest knowledge was absolute existence, and since the roots of Jahmism and the denial of the Lord’s attributes were derived from the Sabians, and since these adherents of the unity of existence were originally Jahmīs, while the elements of Christianity in them—Christianity being akin to Sabianism—created an affinity between them and the Sabians, their object of worship and their god became

---

## Page 474

absolute existence, which they claimed was Allah, echoing the position of many ancient philosophers who denied the Maker and affirmed absolute existence, thereby making Pharaoh’s words valid:

“And what is the Lord of the worlds?”

Although the Muslim philosophers do not agree with this, but acknowledge the Lord from whom the world emanated, their exaltation of absolute existence brought them into agreement and drew them close to one another.

Anyone who considers the statements of al-Nusayr al-Tūsī, the Sabian philosopher; those of al-Sadr al-Qūnawī, the Christian philosopher who espoused the unity of existence; and those of the Ismailis in al-Balāgh al-Akbar and al-Nāmūs al-A‘zam—where it is stated, “The philosophers are the people closest to us. There is no disagreement between us and them except concerning the Necessary Existent: they acknowledge Him, whereas we deny Him”—will recognize the affinity between these people.

Likewise, anyone who examines the correspondence between al-Sadr and al-Nusayr, concerning al-Nusayr’s affirmation of the Necessary Existent according to the method of the Sabian philosophers, and al-Sadr’s identification of this with absolute rather than particular existence, which he claimed was Allah, will understand the truth of what I have said and how they agree in misguidance and unbelief. He will also understand that al-Nusayr is closer to the truth insofar as he acknowledges the Lord, the Maker, who is distinct from creation, but is further sunk in unbelief insofar as he is far removed from prophethood, revealed laws, and worship.

Al-Sadr, on the other hand, is closer to the truth insofar as he venerates acts of worship and prophethood, and practises religious devotion in the Christian manner. Yet he is further sunk in unbelief insofar as his object of worship has no reality: he worships only absolute existence, which has no reality outside the mind.

---

## Page 475

And for this reason, al-Sadr was further sunk in unbelief in his words but less so in his deeds, whereas al-Nusayr was further sunk in unbelief in his deeds but less so in his words. Both, however, were unbelievers in word and deed.

For this reason, Muslims of sound judgment can see that al-Sadr’s words are falsehood, fabrication, and deception, contrary to what the Messenger brought, just as they can see that al-Nusayr’s deeds constitute a breaking away from and rejection of what the Messenger brought.

For this reason, al-Nusayr was closer to the scholars, because some of his statements are true, just as al-Sadr is closer to the worshippers, because some of his deeds are acts of worship.

---

## Page 476

And he said:

Section

People have differed concerning this station—the ultimate goal sought by Allah’s servants.

A group of philosophers and others like them suppose that the soul’s perfection lies in knowledge alone. They identify the knowledge through which it attains perfection with what they themselves know of metaphysics. They regard acts of worship as exercises that refine the soul’s character until it becomes prepared for knowledge. The soul thus becomes a separate world, parallel to the existing world. These people are misguided—indeed, unbelievers—for several reasons.

One is that they believe perfection consists in knowledge alone, just as Jahm, al-Sālihī, al-Ash'arī—according to the better-known of his two views—and most of his followers believed that faith consists in knowledge alone. Yet these philosophers are in a worse position than the Jahmiyyah: the Jahmiyyah identify faith with knowledge of Allah, whereas those philosophers locate the soul’s perfection in its knowledge of absolute existence, considered simply as existence.

What is absolute, when conditioned by its absoluteness, exists only in minds, not among concrete entities. What is absolute, considered without any condition, likewise exists in external reality only as something particular. Even if they know universal existence, divided into necessary and contingent existence, the object of their knowledge has no

---

## Page 477

existence in external reality. The same applies to those who pursue Sufism and devotion according to their method, such as Ibn 'Arabī, Ibn Sab'īn, and others like them.

Moreover, the Jahmiyyah acknowledge the messengers and what they brought. Thus, in general, they acknowledge that Allah created the heavens and the earth, along with other teachings brought by the messengers—unlike the philosophers.

In short, the soul’s perfection does not lie in knowledge alone. Knowledge of Allah must be accompanied by love for Him, worship of Him, and turning to Him. These constitute the soul’s action and volition, and indicate its knowledge and understanding.

The second reason is that they supposed that the knowledge through which the soul attains perfection was their own knowledge, much of which is ignorance rather than knowledge.

The third is that they did not know the divine knowledge brought by the messengers. This is the highest knowledge, through which the soul attains perfection when it acts in accordance with its requirements.

The fourth is that they believe that, once they acquire that knowledge, the obligations of the religious law cease to apply to them and its prohibitions become permissible for them. This is the way of the esoteric sects among the Ismā'īliyyah and others, such as Abū Ya'qūb al-Sijistānī, author of al-Aqālīd al-Malakūtiyyah, and his followers. It is also the way of those heretical Sufis who agree with them and interpret His words:

“And worship your Lord until certainty comes to you.”

They take this to mean that you act until you acquire knowledge, and once you acquire knowledge, action is no longer required of you.

Al-Junayd was told that some people say they pray along the path of righteousness until obligatory duties cease to apply to them and forbidden acts become permissible for them—or words to that effect. He replied:

“Fornication, theft, and drinking wine are better than this.”

---

## Page 478

Among these people are those whose pursuit of unveiling and similar forms of knowledge exceeds their pursuit of what Allah has made obligatory upon them. They say in their supplication: “O Allah, I ask You to safeguard me, in my movements, stillness, steps, intentions, and words, from doubts, conjectures, volition, and delusions that veil hearts from beholding the unseen.”

The underlying issue is that the capacity they regard as perfection is one form of capacity. Another group holds that perfection lies in power, authority, and control over existence—in making commands and prohibitions effective, whether through outward rule and governance or through hidden means. Their worship and spiritual striving are directed towards that end. Many of them engage in polytheism and sorcery, worshipping stars and idols so that the devils will help them achieve their aims. These people are further astray and more ignorant than those mentioned before them. The utmost that someone who worships Allah in pursuit of extraordinary feats attains is a share of this. Thus, some of them are seen flying, others are seen walking, and others … (1).

Among them are ignorant people who have gone astray. Another group regards perfection as a combination of both things. They therefore engage in words and deeds involving polytheism and sorcery, seeking the devils’ help in obtaining information about unseen matters and in making their control over the world effective.

The clear truth is that human perfection consists in worshipping Allah through knowledge and action, as his Lord has commanded him.

> **Footnotes:**
> (1) The original contains two words that the copyist could not decipher.

---

## Page 479

and these are Allah’s servants: the believers and Muslims, Allah’s God-fearing friends, His successful party, and His victorious army. They are people of beneficial knowledge and righteous deeds, those who have purified and perfected their souls, bringing both their faculty of thought and knowledge and their faculty of will and action to perfection. As Allah Most High says:

“Remember Our servants Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob, men of strength and insight.”

Allah Most High also says:

“By the star when it descends.”
“Your companion has neither gone astray nor erred.”
“Nor does he speak from his own desire.”
“It is nothing but revelation revealed.”

Allah Most High also says:

“Guide us to the straight path.”
“The path of those You have blessed, not those who have incurred Your anger, nor those who have gone astray.”

Allah Most High also says:

“If guidance comes to you from Me, then whoever follows My guidance will neither go astray nor fall into misery.”

Allah Most High also says:

“They are guided by their Lord, and it is they who are successful.”

Allah Most High also says:

“To Him ascends good speech, and righteous deeds raise it.”

Allah Most High also says:

“Except those who believe, do righteous deeds, urge one another to uphold the truth, and urge one another to be patient.”

---

## Page 480

He also said:

Section

The doctrine of those who advocate union—such as the author of al-Fusūs and others like him—in its true nature, as implied by their words and explicitly stated in places, is that realities conform to beliefs. This is one of the positions of the sophists: whatever anyone says or believes is true in the mind of the person who says or believes it. Thus, they make falsehood true and say that the spiritual knower declares no one a liar, since falsehood, too, is something that exists and is true in the liar’s mind. If he believes it, it is true in both his belief and his speech. Even if he says something he does not believe, it is true in his speech alone.

For this reason, the adept commands that one believe everything that people believe, as he said:

“People have formed beliefs concerning God;
And I have embraced all that they believed.”

It is well known that the objects of contradictory beliefs do not exist in external reality, but only in the mind in which those beliefs are held.

For this reason, they command the simultaneous affirmation of contradictory and contrary propositions, making this one of the foundations of their path and their realization. It is well known that two contradictory propositions cannot both hold true in external reality. Yet it is possible to believe that they do, so that this is true in the believer’s mind. They claim that this comes about through spiritual unveiling; thus, their unveiling is contradictory. I confronted one of them with this, and he said:

“Both of them

---

## Page 481

are true, as in the case of someone to whom it was unveiled that Venus is above Mercury, and another to whom it was unveiled that it is below Mercury.” He said, “According to this man’s unveiling, it is above Mercury, while according to that man’s unveiling, it is below Mercury,” and likewise in similar cases. Thus, they made established realities conform to spiritual unveiling, belief, and speech.

[For this reason they say:
“Go wherever you wish, for Allah is there;
And say whatever you wish concerning Him, for Allah is the All-Encompassing.”] (*).

The implication of this principle is that everyone may say whatever he wishes and believe whatever he wishes, without distinguishing between truth and falsehood, or between a truthful person and a liar, and that nothing in existence is to be rejected. This is indeed what they say.

This concerns statements and knowledge. As for commands and actions, their adept says:

“Nothing is forbidden for us. But these veiled people said, ‘It is forbidden,’ so we said, ‘It is forbidden for you.’”

Thus, they acknowledge neither command nor prohibition, as the judge who was a disciple of the author of al-Fusūs said in verses recited to me by the witness Ibn ‘Amad, known as ‘Ar‘ayh (1):

“All things form but a single order … containing neither praise nor blame;
Only custom, innate disposition, and the Lawgiver … assign distinctions in judgment.”

Accordingly, nothing remains for words and deeds but mere power. This is why they always go along with whatever occurs: whatever exists and takes place is, in their view, true. Thus, the lawful is whatever you find and whatever comes into your possession; the unlawful is whatever you declare unlawful; truth is whatever you say, whatever it may be; and falsehood is whatever no one has said.

These people are worse than the libertine heretics who follow sheer predestination. For those people negate commands and prohibitions, reward and punishment, whereas these

> **Footnotes:**
> (1) This is how the letters appear in the original.
> 
> (*) Shaykh Nāsir ibn Hamad al-Fahd writes (p. 21):
> This is a verse in the basīt metre, printed in this form in al-Majmū‘. The correct layout is:
> (For this reason they say:
> Go wherever you wish, for Allah is there, and say … whatever you wish concerning Him, for Allah is the All-Encompassing.

---

## Page 482

have also negated the Creator, the prophetic message, and all realities. They have made realities depend on what is unveiled to a person, without recognizing that realities have an intrinsic nature by which something is established as real and by whose contrary it is negated. Rather, in their view, being unrestricted entails this: you must not confine yourself to any one belief, but must embrace everything that people have believed. Even if these are contradictory statements, existence encompasses them all, and the oneness of existence encompasses them all.

Yet it is well known that existence accommodates only the existence of these beliefs, not the actual reality of everything believed. Rational people do not dispute this. A false belief and an untrue statement exist and are part of existence, but this does not entail that they are true or truthful. When truth and truthfulness are ascribed to statements reporting something, what is meant is not merely that those statements exist. Their existence is known through the senses; on that interpretation, they would all be true and truthful.

It is well known that someone asking whether such statements are true and truthful regards them as falling into categories of truth and falsehood, truthfulness and untruthfulness. The question is whether or not they correspond to what is reported. A statement may correspond to the speaker’s belief but not to external reality: this is error. It may sometimes be called falsehood, and sometimes it is not described that way.

Examples of the former include the Prophet’s words, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“Abū al-Sanābil has spoken falsely.”

And his words:

“Whoever said that has spoken falsely. He will indeed receive two rewards. He is indeed one who strives hard”—a fighter in Allah’s cause.

Also, the words of ʿUbāda, “Your father has spoken falsely,” and the words of Ibn ʿAbbās, “Nawf has spoken falsely.”

---

## Page 483

And the latter is illustrated by the words of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“I have not forgotten, nor has the prayer been shortened.” Dhū al-Yadayn said to him, “Yes, you have forgotten.”

The distinction—and Allah knows best—seems to be that someone who makes a statement while neglecting the means of determining whether he is right or wrong, and consequently errs, is called a liar, unlike someone who has not been negligent. This is because (1) he spoke recklessly, without proof or evidence, and erred, unlike someone who reports something without negligence.

“This distinction can also be applied to someone who swears to something, believing it to be as he has sworn, but it proves otherwise: if he swears recklessly, without any basis to rely upon—for example, someone who swears that this is a crow, or that it is not a crow, without any supporting grounds whatsoever, and is then shown to be mistaken—[then this person breaks his oath, and that person breaks his oath just as this one does, even if he is unaware of his error and even if he is correct; this is the question of his swearing that he is in Paradise]. This is like saying that a mufti who gives a ruling without knowledge sins even if he is correct. The same applies to someone who prays towards the direction of prayer without making an effort to determine it, and to someone who interprets the Quran according to his own opinion.” (*)

For this reason, you find these people among the most untruthful in their reports. Indeed, lying is no different from telling the truth in their eyes, so they use it as needed and do not mind making two contradictory statements about the same thing. You also find that their actions follow their desires: they likewise perform contradictory deeds when one happens to take priority at one time and the other at another.

They always side with whoever commands obedience, whether he is a believer or an unbeliever, righteous or wicked, a man of utmost truthfulness or a heretic. Although the Tatars before their conversion to Islam shared this trait with them, they were better than them in matters of assertion. The Tatars do not make two contradictory assertions about divine matters. Rather, one of them either believes something on the basis of knowledge or imitation, or holds no belief at all. But as for combining

What this passage means—and Allah knows best—is that anyone who swears recklessly, without any basis to rely upon, breaks his oath even if he is unaware of his error, and even if he is correct.

## Page 484

two contradictory assertions—they do not do so. Thus, these people are in a worse condition than the Tatars and their like. This is why they have no good final outcome: they are not God-fearing people who distinguish between what is commanded and what is forbidden, or between truth and falsehood. The good final outcome belongs only to the God-fearing. Each of them maintains his position only insofar as he possesses power. It is well known that his power does not last; rather, he uses it to perform deeds that bring about his ruin.

There is no doubt that these people fall under the words of Allah Most High:

“Those who disbelieve and turn others away from the way of Allah—He has rendered their deeds worthless.”

And His words:

“That is because those who disbelieve follow falsehood.”

And His words:

“As for those who disbelieve, their deeds are like a mirage in a lowland which a thirsty person thinks is water, until, when he reaches it, he finds nothing there, but finds Allah there, who settles his account in full.”

And His words:

“The deeds of those who disbelieve in their Lord are like ashes scattered by a fierce wind on a stormy day. They can gain nothing from what they have earned.”

And His words:

“Deaf, dumb and blind, they do not understand.”

And His words:

“We have certainly created for Hell many of the jinn and mankind. They have hearts with which they do not understand, eyes with which they do not see, and ears with which they do not hear. They are like cattle; rather, they are even further astray.”

[There is no doubt that truth is of two kinds: truth as an existing reality, to which truthful reports relate; and truth as an objective to be sought, to which wise commands and righteous deeds relate. The opposite of truth is falsehood. Falsehood in the second sense is illustrated by the words of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“Every amusement in which a man engages is futile, except shooting with his bow, training his horse, and playing with his wife, for these are worthwhile.”

When an existing reality is reported contrary to what it is, the report is a lie.] (*)

These people do not distinguish between truth and falsehood: between the existing reality that should be believed and the nonexistent falsehood that should be denied in reports

---

## Page 485

about either of them; nor between truth as an objective to be sought, which should be embraced, and falsehood, which should be avoided. Rather, they seek whichever of the two they desire and can attain.

The surest truth as an existing reality is what Allah has declared to exist; and the report concerning truth as an objective to be sought is what Allah has commanded. Alternatively, you may say: the most truthful report concerning existing reality is Allah’s report, and the best command concerning truth as an objective to be sought is Allah’s command. Faith encompasses these two foundations: believing what He has reported and obeying what He has commanded.

When the two are mentioned together, it is said:

“Indeed, those who believe and do righteous deeds.”

Deeds are better than words, as al-Hasan al-Basrī said:

“Faith is neither wishful thinking nor outward adornment; rather, it is what becomes firmly established in the heart and is confirmed by deeds.”

---

## Page 486

The shaykh was also asked:

About a group who had come together in various forms of corruption, each clinging to some pretext. Among them is someone who says that Yūnus al-Qattāt will deliver his followers and disciples from a grievous reckoning and painful punishment. Another alleges that Alī al-Harīrī had been granted such a state that, when alone with women and beardless youths, his genitals would become those of a woman. Another claims prophethood and maintains that he must inevitably appear at some time, whereupon his religion and religious law will prevail. His wicked religious law includes forbidding women, permitting the abomination of sodomy, and forbidding certain foods and other things, such as figs, almonds, and lemons.

A group followed him, including some who used to pray but abandoned prayer. A particular set of people meets with him on many days, etc.

He replied:

As for the claim that Yūnus al-Qattātī will deliver his followers and disciples from a grievous reckoning and painful torment on the Day of Resurrection:

---

## Page 487

the general answer is as follows:

Whoever claims that any shaykh will deliver his disciples from punishment on the Day of Resurrection has thereby claimed that his shaykh is superior to Muhammad ibn Abd Allāh, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. Whoever says this must be called upon to repent; if he repents, well and good; otherwise, he is to be put to death.

For it is established in an authentic hadith that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“O Fātimah, daughter of Muhammad, I cannot protect you at all against Allah. O Safiyyah, aunt of Allah’s Messenger, I cannot protect you at all against Allah. O Abbās, uncle of Allah’s Messenger, I cannot protect you at all against Allah. Ask me for whatever you wish of my wealth.”

It is also established in an authentic collection that he said:

“Let me not find any of you coming on the Day of Resurrection with a bellowing camel upon his neck, saying, ‘O Messenger of Allah, help me!’ I will say, ‘I cannot protect you at all against Allah. I have conveyed the message to you.’”—referring to the hadith in full.

Similar statements are reported in other sayings. If Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, says such things to his household and his Companions among the Emigrants and the Helpers, who believed in him, honored him, and supported him—saying that he cannot protect them at all against Allah—how can such a claim be made about a shaykh whose highest possible standing is to be among those who follow them in righteousness?

Allah Most High has said:

“And what will make you realize what the Day of Judgment is?”

“Again, what will make you realize what the Day of Judgment is?”

“A Day when no soul will have any power to help another; all command that Day belongs to Allah.”

And He said:

“And fear a Day when no soul will avail another in any way.”

There are other texts of the Quran and Sunnah to the same effect. It is known that, on the Day of Resurrection, the prophets and others can do nothing beyond intercession.

It is established in an authentic collection that “People will come to Adam seeking his intercession, and he will say, ‘Myself! Myself!’ Noah, Abraham, Moses, and Jesus will say the same—these are the messengers of firm resolve—

---

## Page 488

and they are the best of creation. Jesus will say to them, ‘Go to Muhammad, a servant whose past and future sins Allah has forgiven.’ When I see my Lord, I will fall down before Him in prostration. He will say, ‘O Muhammad, raise your head. Speak, and you will be heard; ask, and you will be given; intercede, and your intercession will be accepted.’ He will specify a group for me, and I will admit them to Paradise.” Something similar is mentioned concerning the second occasion.

Thus, the best of creation and the most honored of them before Allah does not intercede upon seeing his Lord until he has prostrated before Him and praised Him. Then Allah permits him to intercede and specifies a group for him to admit to Paradise. This confirms His statement, Most High:

“Who is it that can intercede with Him except by His permission?”

There are other verses to the same effect.

An authentic hadith states that the angels, the prophets, and the believers will intercede, but only with His permission and within specified limits. The matter is not left to the intercessor’s discretion.

This concerns someone who is known to intercede. If someone were to say, “Muhammad will deliver all his disciples from the Fire,” he would be lying. Rather, some people from his community will enter the Fire, and he will then intercede for them. As for the shaykhs, their intercession is not like his. Allah may accept a righteous man’s intercession on behalf of whomever He wills, and intercession is only for those who have faith.

As for those who affiliate themselves with Shaykh Yūnus, many of them are disbelievers in Allah and His Messenger. They do not acknowledge the obligation of the five daily prayers, fasting the month of Ramadan, or pilgrimage to the Ancient House, nor do they regard as forbidden what Allah and His Messenger have forbidden. Indeed, those acquainted with them know the statements they make reviling Allah, His Messenger, the Quran, and Islam.

---

## Page 489

As for their ordinary followers who do not know their secrets or their true nature, they retain the Islam of ordinary Muslims, which they learned from other Muslims, not from these people. Their inner circle, however—such as Shaykh Salūl, Jahlān, al-Sahbānī, and others—did not regard prayer as obligatory; indeed, they did not even acknowledge the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, as a Messenger.

Their poetry, such as that of al-Kūjlī and others, contains abuse of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and of the Quran and Islam that neither Jews nor Christians would approve of. Some attribute this poetry to Yūnus, while others say that it has been falsely attributed to him. Yet it is known and witnessed that they recite verses of disbelief and work themselves into ecstatic states over them. One of them urinates into the food and says, “Yūnus soothes my heart,” or, “Yūnus’s rosewater.” They consider food containing urine lawful and regard this as a blessing.

Among their statements of disbelief are such verses as:

“And I guarded the sanctuary, and I dwelt within it;
I left creation along the paths of bewilderment.
Moses, on Mount Sinai, when he fell down before me, spoke to me in secret.
Wa-sāhib aqrab anā jannabūh, until the Day of Resurrection came;
he sees creation going in throngs to his prophet Jesus,
who attends to their needs.”

They also say:

“Come, let us destroy the congregational mosque and turn it into a jamāra;
let us break the wood of the pulpit and make a non-Muslim’s girdle from it;
let us burn sheets of paper and make a lute from them;
let us pluck out the judge’s beard and make its strings from it.
I charged against the Throne until it rang out;
I shouted at Muhammad until he hajja;
the seven seas tremble in awe of me.”

---

## Page 490

And there are other matters graver than these—too grave to mention, for they contain disbelief worse than the claim of those who say that Allah has a son.

As for the claim that one of the shaykhs had his genitals change into those of a woman, this is a fabricated lie. Rather, his path contains reprehensible practices contrary to Islam, which anyone familiar with Islam would recognize. His followers transmit and record statements of disbelief from him, such as: “If I killed seventy prophets, I would not be in error.” It is well known that killing even one prophet is among the gravest acts of disbelief. In a hadith traced back to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, he said:

“The people most severely punished on the Day of Resurrection will be those who killed a prophet or were killed by a prophet.”

If it is said, “He said this while witnessing the reality of divine decree governing creation—that Allah is the Creator of His servants’ deeds,” then the excuse is uglier than the sin. For if divine decree were a valid excuse, neither Iblīs nor Pharaoh nor any other disbeliever would incur blame in this world or the Hereafter. Yet if someone wronged this person who invokes divine decree as an excuse, he would fight him and become angry with him. If divine decree is a valid excuse, then it excuses that person’s doing whatever he wishes to him; and if it is not an excuse, he would not harm a human being. How, then, can it be an excuse for someone who disbelieves in Allah and His Messenger?

Adam, peace be upon him, prevailed over Moses in argument only because Moses blamed him for the calamity that had befallen him, not for the sin insofar as it violated the right of Allah Most High. Adam had repented, and whoever repents of a sin is like someone who has no sin. Rather, Moses said to him, “What did you do to cause us and yourself to be expelled from Paradise?” Adam replied, “Do you blame me for something Allah decreed for me forty years before I was created?” Thus Adam prevailed over Moses in argument.

---

## Page 491

Likewise, anyone who suffers a calamity on account of his father or anyone else is commanded to submit to Allah’s decree, as Allah Most High says:

“And whoever believes in Allah, He will guide his heart.”

ʿAlqama said: “This is the man who is struck by a calamity and knows that it is from Allah, so he accepts it and submits.”

As for sins, the servant must refrain from committing them; if he does commit them, he must repent of them. Whoever repents and feels remorse resembles his father Adam, while whoever persists and invokes divine decree as an excuse resembles his enemy Iblīs.

Allah Most High says:

“So be patient. Indeed, Allah’s promise is true. And seek forgiveness for your sin.”

Thus, the believer is commanded to bear calamities patiently and seek forgiveness for sins and faults.

---

## Page 492

Section

As for the man who claims prophethood, declares the abomination of sodomy lawful, forbids marriage, and holds the other views mentioned, his case is too obvious to require discussion. He is one of the unbelievers and among the vilest apostates. Killing him and those who follow him is obligatory by the consensus of the Muslims.

Each of these people must either be addressed with evidence, in the hope that Allah will accept his repentance and guide him, or have the prescribed punishment carried out upon him and be put to death. Whoever is capable of either course must undertake it. As for whoever is incapable of either, Allah does not burden any soul beyond its capacity. Nevertheless, he must recognize what is right and love it, reject what is wrong and hate it, and do whatever he can to enjoin right and forbid wrong, as the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said in the authentic hadith:

“Whoever among you sees a wrong, let him change it with his hand. If he cannot, then with his tongue. If he cannot, then with his heart. Beyond that there is not even an atom’s weight of faith.”

And Allah, glorified and exalted is He, knows best.

---

## Page 493

We request, as a kindness from Shaykh al-Islam, the mufti of mankind, Taqī al-Dīn—may Allah reward him with Paradise—that he give us a ruling concerning two men who disputed the following two lines of verse:

The Lord is real, and the servant is real … Would that I knew: who is bound by duty?
If you say, ‘The servant,’ he is dead … If you say, ‘The Lord,’ how could He be bound by duty?

One of the two men said:
“This statement is unbelief, for its author has made the Lord and the servant a single reality, with no distinction between them, and has nullified religious obligation.”

The second man replied:
“You have not understood the meaning, and you have accused the author of something he neither believed nor intended. He said, ‘The Lord is real, and the servant is real,’ meaning that the Lord is real in His lordship, and the servant is real in his servitude. The Lord is not a servant, nor is the servant a lord, as you alleged.

“He then said, ‘Would that I knew: who is bound by duty?’ while knowing that religious obligation is real. He was perplexed as to whom to attribute its fulfilment, and so he said, ‘If you say, “The servant,” he is dead.’ A dead man cannot move of his own accord; rather, someone else turns him as he wishes. The same is true of the servant—even if he is

---

## Page 494

alive—his relationship with his Lord is like that of the deceased with the person washing his body: he can perform no action of his own independently of Allah. For if He, glorified be He, did not give the servant strength to fulfil his obligations, the servant would be unable to do so. Thus, the action belongs to Allah in reality, and to the servant figuratively. The evidence for this is the saying, ‘There is no power nor strength except through Allah, the Most High, the Most Great’—meaning that there is no power to turn away from disobedience, nor strength to obey, except through Allah.

“It is known that the Lord is under no obligation, since no one imposes obligations upon Him, and that the servant fulfils the obligations imposed upon him only through Allah. Religious obligation is real. Upon witnessing this state, the speaker marvelled and became perplexed, while acknowledging religious obligation and that it truly rests upon the servant. No reasonable person should therefore disparage someone whose words he does not understand; rather, the failing lies in his own limited understanding.”

Which of the two is right?

Shaykh al-Islam Ibn Taymiyyah—may Allah sanctify his soul and illuminate his tomb—answered:

Praise be to Allah. This second man’s words are false. He ventures into something of which he has no comprehensive knowledge and whose true nature he does not understand. He neither understands the true nature of Ibn ʿArabī’s statement and the underlying principle from which this poetry and other statements derive, nor accepts what this wording entails and signifies.

Ibn ʿArabī’s underlying principle is that existence is one, and that necessary existence is identical with contingent existence. He also maintains that the nonexistent is a thing, that the entities of nonexistent things are fixed in nonexistence, and that the existence of the Real governs them. Thus, in his view, the existence of everything is identical with the existence of the Real. This has been discussed at length elsewhere.

---

## Page 495

For this reason he said:

“Since Pharaoh held the position of authority, being the ruler of his time and the caliph by the sword—even if he was unjust according to the conventions of religious law—he therefore said:

‘I am your lord, the Most High.’

“That is: even if all are lords in some respect, I am the highest among them by virtue of the outward authority over you that I have been given. When the sorcerers recognized the truth of what he said, they did not deny it, but acknowledged it. They said to him:

‘So decree whatever you are to decree. You can only decree for this worldly life.’

“Sovereignty belongs to you. Thus Pharaoh’s statement, ‘I am your lord, the Most High,’ was correct, even if he was identical with the Real.”

He said:

“Among His Most Beautiful Names is the Most High. Above whom, when there is none but Him? And above what, when He is none other than Himself?”

He continued, eventually saying:

“Whoever understands what we have established concerning numbers, and that negating them is identical with affirming them, knows that the Real, declared free of all likeness, is creation, described in terms of likeness. Thus the one who commands—the Creator—is the created, and the created command is the Creator. All this comes from one essence; indeed, it is that one essence.”

He also said:

“Do you not see that the Real manifests Himself through the attributes of creation? All the attributes of the Real truly belong to it, just as the attributes of things brought into existence truly belong to the Creator.”

Statements of this kind abound in his writings. This man follows a progression in his spiritual path similar to that of the heretical Qarmatians. What first appears is the belief of the Mu'tazilites of the Kullābiyyah, who deny the divine attributes known through revelation while affirming the seven or eight attributes. Then comes the belief of the philosophers, who deny the attributes and affirm an abstract necessary existence from which contingent beings emanated.

---

## Page 496

Then, beyond this, that existence is identified with the existence of every being. For him, there are not two existences, one necessary and the other contingent; nor is one the Creator and the other the created. Rather, necessary existence itself is identical with contingent existence itself, despite the multiplicity of levels. For him, these levels are the fixed entities in nonexistence, as alleged by those who say that the nonexistent is a thing. Undoubtedly, anyone who regards the nonexistent as a thing with a fixed reality outside the mind holds a false position.

Those people, however, say that the Creator has given these entities a created existence. Ibn ‘Arabī says instead: His very existence emanated upon them. They therefore depend upon Him in respect of His existence, while He depends upon their fixed reality. Hence his words:

“He worships me, and I worship Him;
He praises me, and I praise Him.”

For this reason, the imposition of obligations is impossible in his view, since obligations must be imposed by one party upon another: one commands, and the other is commanded. Thus, the imposition of obligations is impossible.

The same point is illustrated, with respect to speech and hearing, by the words of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“Allah has pardoned my community for what they say within themselves, so long as they do not utter it or act upon it.”

Since the one speaking here is himself the one spoken to, he made this an analogy for the Lord’s existence. In his view, every utterance in existence is His speech, and He is both the speaker and the listener.

Hence he says:

“If you say, ‘A servant,’ then he is dead.”

Elsewhere, I saw it in his own handwriting:

---

## Page 497

“If you say, ‘A servant,’ then that is negation,” because, in his view, the servant has no created existence; rather, his existence is the necessary, pre-eternal existence itself. This has been discussed at length elsewhere, for the man’s statements explain one another. This principle—the doctrine of the unity of existence—is his position, as well as that of Ibn Sab‘īn, his companion al-Shushtarī, al-Tilimsānī, al-Sadr al-Qūnawī, Sa‘īd al-Farghānī, ‘Abd Allāh al-Balyānī, Ibn al-Fārid, the author of Nazm al-Sulūk (The Poem of the Spiritual Path), and other heretics who profess unity, indwelling, and union.

As for the meaning of this poetry, his words, “If only I knew: who is subject to obligations?” are a rhetorical question denying that anyone is subject to obligations. He then says, “If you say, ‘A servant,’ then he is dead,” while elsewhere he says, “then that is negation.” Both are false: the servant exists and has a real being; he is not nonexistent or devoid of reality. Rather, Allah is the One who gave him existence and reality. This is the religion of the Muslims: everything other than Allah is created by Allah and exists because Allah has given it existence. Nothing exists except through Allah’s bringing it into existence; considered in itself, it has no claim to anything but nonexistence (1) . . . existent, living, speaking, acting, willing, and capable; rather, all this . . . (2) does not preclude the reality of their entities, attributes, and actions.

> **Footnotes:**
> (1, 2) A blank space in the original.

---

## Page 498

For it is He—glorified be He—who made the living alive. Indeed, it is He who made the Muslim a Muslim and the worshipper one who prays, as Abraham, Allah’s close friend, said:

“Our Lord, make us both submissive to You.”

And he said:

“My Lord, make me an establisher of prayer, and also some of my descendants.”

This concerns the creation of the servants’ actions, which is the doctrine of Ahl al-Sunnah wa-l-Jamā‘ah. They nevertheless agree that the servant is subject to commands and prohibitions, rewarded and punished, given promises and warnings. It is He—glorified be He—who made the white white, the black black, the tall tall, the short short, the moving moving, the stationary stationary, the moist moist, the dry dry, the male male, the female female, the sweet sweet, and the bitter bitter. Nevertheless, these entities possess these attributes, and Allah Most High is the Creator of the entities and their attributes. What, then, is surprising about a created entity possessing its attributes? And on what basis is Allah the Creator of all this in truth?

Thus, if someone says, “The Lord is real, and the servant is real,” and means that one reality is identical with the other, this is union and heresy, and it is this that is incompatible with being subject to obligations. But if he means that the servant is a real, created being whom the Creator created, this is the position of the Muslims. That does not preclude the Creator from enabling the creature, just as He creates him.

His statement, “If you say, ‘A servant,’ then he is dead,” is false. The servant is not dead; rather, he is alive, and Allah Most High has given him life, as He Most High says:

“How can you disbelieve in Allah when you were lifeless and He brought you to life?”

Allah does not impose obligations upon the dead; He imposes them only upon the living. If it is said that by “dead” he meant that the servant, considered in himself, has no life, the response is: interpreting his intended meaning in this way is unsound both in wording and in meaning. As for the wording, his words do not imply this. As for the meaning, if his words are interpreted in this way, they do not preclude being subject to obligations.

---

## Page 499

Thus, if he would be dead were it not for Allah’s giving him life, and Allah has indeed given him life, then he has become alive through Allah’s giving him life. Accordingly, Allah has imposed obligations upon a living person, not a dead one.

As for the statements of the heretics’ brethren and their defenders, they claim that he said, “If only I knew who is subject to obligations!” while knowing that these obligations are real, but was perplexed as to whom to attribute their fulfilment. So he said:

“If you say, ‘A servant,’ then he is dead.”

They say that a dead person has no movement of his own; rather, his movement comes from someone else, who turns him over as he wishes. Likewise, the servant—even though alive—is with his Lord like a dead body with the person washing it: he performs no act of his own independently of Allah.

The answer to them is that this defence is invalid on several grounds.

First, there is no cause for perplexity here. The one subject to obligations is the servant, without doubt or perplexity. It is impossible that Allah should be the one obliged to fast, circumambulate the Kaaba, or cast pebbles at the pillars. Rather, He is the one who commands these acts, and the servant is the one commanded to perform them. Whoever is perplexed as to whether Allah or the servant is commanded to do these things is either unsound of mind—a madman—or corrupt in religion—a heretic and a zindīq.

Allah’s being the Creator of the servant and his actions does not preclude the servant from being the one subject to commands and prohibitions. No one has ever said that Allah is the one who bows, prostrates, circumambulates the Kaaba, casts pebbles at the pillars, or fasts the month of Ramadan. Rather, the entire Muslim community agrees that it is the servant who bows, prostrates, fasts, and worships. There is no disagreement about this between Ahl al-Sunnah and the Qadariyyah.

Second, his statement that the servant—even though alive—is with his Lord like a dead body with the person washing it is incorrect. A dead person has neither sensation nor volition concerning what takes place

---

## Page 500

in his body in the form of movement, nor does he have any power over it. Nor can he be described as loving or hating an action, willing it or being averse to it, or as bowing, prostrating, fasting, performing Hajj, or fighting the enemy.

As for the statement of those who maintain this view:

A dead person is neither praised nor blamed for the actions of the person washing him, nor is he rewarded or punished. The servant, however, has been made by Allah to be alive, endowed with will and power, and capable of acting. He fasts, prays, performs Hajj, kills, and engages in illicit sexual intercourse by his own choice and will. Allah is the Creator of his being, his attributes, and his actions. He has a will, and Allah is the Creator of his will, as the Most High says:

“For whoever among you wills to follow the straight path.”

“And you do not will unless Allah, Lord of the worlds, wills.”

He has power, and Allah is the Creator of his power. He prays, fasts, and performs Hajj and Umrah, while Allah is his Creator and the Creator of his actions. Thus, likening him to a dead person is a false analogy.

Third, it may be said: If he is like a dead person with the one washing him, then the one washing him would be the one subject to obligations. Allah would therefore be the one subject to obligations. It would consequently follow that the Lord is the one subject to obligations.

Fourth, all rational human beings agree, in accordance with the innate disposition Allah has given them, that a living servant is commanded and forbidden, and is praised and blamed for his voluntary actions. They also agree that anyone who invokes divine decree to excuse his wrongdoing and indecencies has no acceptable excuse. If someone wronged another person, no one would accept his invoking divine decree to absolve himself of blame. As for a dead person, no rational person blames him, commands him, or forbids him. How, then, can the one be compared to the other?

As for the statement:

“If Allah did not give the servant the strength to fulfil his obligations, he would be unable to do so,”

---

## Page 501

this is a correct statement; however, nothing in it precludes the servant from being subject to obligations, commands, and prohibitions, or from praying, fasting, killing, or committing illicit sexual intercourse.

As for his statement:

“Action belongs to Allah in a real sense, and to the servant only metaphorically.”

This statement is false. Rather, it is the servant who truly prays, fasts, performs Hajj and Umrah, and believes; and it is he who truly disbelieves, acts wickedly, kills, commits illicit sexual intercourse, and steals. Allah Most High is not described by any of these attributes; rather, He is exalted above them. Yet He is the One who makes the servant perform these actions. They are truly His creations and what He brings about, while also truly being the servant’s actions.

However, a group of speculative theologians who affirm divine decree supposed that an action is identical to what it produces, and that creating is identical to what is created. Thus, when they believed that the servants’ actions were created and brought about by Allah, they said, “They are therefore His actions.”

They were then also asked, “Are they the servant’s actions?” They fell into confusion. Some said, “They are his acquisition, not his action,” without establishing a genuine distinction between acquisition and action. Others said, “Rather, they are an act shared by two agents.” Still others said, “Rather, the Lord brings about the act itself, while the servant brings about its attributes.”

The correct position is that held by the leading authorities of the Sunnah and the majority of the Muslim community: to distinguish between an action and what it produces, and between creating and what is created. The servants’ actions, like all other things that come into being, are created and brought about by Allah, just as the servant himself and all his attributes are created and brought about by Allah. These are not His act of creating and acting itself; rather, they are what is created and brought about. These actions are the servant’s actions, subsisting in him. They do not subsist in Allah, nor is He characterized by them, for He is not characterized by His creations or by what He brings about;

---

## Page 502

rather, He is characterized by His act of creating and His action, just as He is characterized by all else that subsists in His Essence. The servant performs these actions and is the one characterized by them. He has the power to perform them and does so by his own choice and will. All of this is created by Allah. Thus, they are the servant’s actions and what the Lord brings about.

These attributes, however, were not created by Allah through the servant’s power and will, unlike his voluntary actions, which He created through His creation of the servant’s will and power, just as He created other effects through other causes. This is discussed at length elsewhere, but this is as much as this sheet allows. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 503

What do the esteemed scholars—the imams of the religion and guides of the Muslims—say concerning a book circulating among the people, whose author claims that he composed it and made it public with the permission of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, in a dream he claims to have seen? Most of his book contradicts Allah’s revealed scriptures and runs directly counter to the words of His messengers.

Among the things he says in it is:

“Adam, peace be upon him, was called insān (a human being) only because he is to the Real, Most High, what the insān al-ʿayn (pupil), through which sight occurs, is to the eye.”

Elsewhere he says:

“The Real, declared beyond all likeness, is creation, described in terms of likeness.”

Concerning the people of Noah, peace be upon him, he says that if they had abandoned their worship of Wadd, Suwāʿ, Yaghūth, Yaʿūq, and Nasr:

“They would have been ignorant of the Real to the extent that they abandoned these objects of worship.”

He then says:

“For the Real has an aspect in every object of worship, known to those who know it and unknown to those who do not. Thus, the one who knows knows whom he has worshipped and in what form He appeared, so that he worshipped Him. Differentiation and multiplicity are like the limbs within a perceptible form.”

He then says concerning the people of Hūd, peace be upon him, that they attained nearness itself, so distance disappeared, and consequently what is called Hell ceased to apply to them. They thus attained the bliss of nearness by virtue of their desert, through that which granted them this delightful station of spiritual experience as a favor. They received it only through what their essential realities merited by the deeds they had practised, and they were upon the Lord’s straight path.

---

## Page 504

He then denied in it that the threat of punishment applies to any of Allah’s servants against whom the sentence of punishment has become due. Does one who believes him in this become a disbeliever, or not? And does one who approves of this statement of his become a disbeliever, or not? Does a sane adult who hears it incur sin if he does not reject it with his tongue or in his heart, or not? Give us a clear and explicit ruling, in keeping with the covenant Allah took requiring clarification. Neglect has harmed the weak and the ignorant. Allah is the One whose help we seek, and upon Him we rely to hasten exemplary punishment upon the heretics, so that matters may be set right and the root of misguidance eradicated.

He replied:

All praise is due to Allah.

Every one of these reprehensible statements quoted above constitutes disbelief, beyond dispute among the adherents of the religions—Muslims, Jews and Christians—let alone its being disbelief under the law of Islam.

For the statement:

“Adam is to the Real, Most High, what the pupil, through which sight occurs, is to the eye”

entails that Adam is a part and portion of the Real—exalted and holy is He—and that he is the finest of His parts and portions. This is the true nature of these people’s doctrine, as is well known from their statements.

The second statement agrees with this, namely:

“The Real, declared beyond all likeness, is creation, described in terms of likeness.”

Hence, he continues that passage by saying:

“Thus, the Creator is the created, and the created is the Creator. All of this comes from a single reality. No, rather, it is that single reality, and it is the many realities.

‘Consider, then, what you think.’

‘O my father, do as you are commanded.’

The son is identical to his father, so he did not see that he was slaughtering

---

## Page 505

anyone other than himself. ‘So We ransomed him with a great sacrifice.’ Thus, he who had appeared in human form appeared in the form of a ram. He who is identical to the father appeared in a form, not in the capacity of a son.

‘And He created from it its mate.’

Thus, he married none other than himself.”

Elsewhere, he said:

“He is hidden from every understanding except that of whoever says that the world is His form and His identity.”

He also said:

“Among His most beautiful names is ‘the Most High.’ Above whom, when there is none but Him? Above what, when He is none other than Himself? His exaltedness is intrinsic to Him, and in respect of existence He is identical with all existent beings. Thus, what are called originated beings are exalted in themselves and are nothing other than Him.”

He went on to say:

“He is identical with what is manifest, and identical with what is hidden even as He manifests Himself. There is no one besides Him who sees Him, and no one other than Him who speaks of Him. He is manifest to Himself and hidden from Himself—and He is the one called Abū Saʿīd al-Kharrāz—and is called by other names of originated beings.”

He went on to say:

“The one who is Most High in Himself possesses the perfection that encompasses all matters of existence and all relations of nonexistence, whether praiseworthy according to custom, reason, and revealed law, or blameworthy according to custom, reason, and revealed law. This belongs exclusively to the one named Allah.”

He also said:

“Do you not see that the Real manifests Himself with the attributes of originated beings? He has stated this about Himself, including attributes of deficiency and blame. Do you not see that the created being manifests itself with the attributes of the Real? From first to last, these are its attributes, just as the attributes of originated beings truly belong to the Real.”

And there are other statements of this kind. For the author of the aforementioned book, Fusūs al-Hikam (The Bezels of Wisdom), and others like him

---

## Page 506

—such as his companion al-Qūnawī, al-Tilimsānī, Ibn Sab'īn, al-Shushtarī, Ibn al-Fārid, and their followers—hold the doctrine that existence is one. They are called proponents of the unity of existence and claim spiritual realization and gnosis. They identify the Creator’s existence with the very existence of created beings. Thus, whatever qualities created beings possess, good or bad, praiseworthy or blameworthy, are, in their view, qualities of the Creator Himself. According to them, the Creator has no existence whatsoever that is distinct from the existence of created beings and separate from them. Rather, they hold that there is absolutely nothing other than the Creator, nor anything besides Him.

Among their sayings is: “There is nothing but Allah.” Thus, in their view, idol worshippers have worshipped none other than Him, since they believe there is nothing other than Him. For this reason, they interpret His words, exalted is He:

“Your Lord has decreed that you worship none but Him,”

as meaning: “Your Lord has ordained that you will worship none but Him.” For, according to them, there is no other being whose worship could even be conceived; hence, every idol worshipper has worshipped only Allah.

For this reason, the author of this book held that those who worshipped the calf were right, and stated that Moses rebuked Aaron for condemning their worship of the calf. He said:

“Moses knew more about the matter than Aaron, because he knew what the worshippers of the calf had worshipped. He knew that Allah had decreed that they worship none but Him, and whatever Allah decrees necessarily comes to pass. Thus, when the incident occurred, Moses’ rebuke of his brother Aaron concerned his denunciation and his failure to follow. For the gnostic is one who sees the Real in everything—indeed, who sees Him as identical with everything.”

For this reason, they regard Pharaoh as one of the foremost gnostics who attained realization, and hold that he was right in claiming lordship. As he said in this book:

“Since Pharaoh held the position of command as master of his time, and was a tyrant according to the conventions of religious law, he therefore said:

‘I am your most exalted lord.’

---

## Page 507

That is, although all are lords in some respect, I am the highest among them by virtue of the outward authority I have been granted over them. When the sorcerers recognized that Pharaoh spoke truthfully, they did not reject his claim. Rather, they acknowledged it and said to him:

‘So decree whatever you will decree.’

For power is yours. Thus Pharaoh’s statement, ‘I am your most exalted lord,’ was correct, and he was identical with the Real.”

It is enough to show you their unbelief that among their least extreme claims is that Pharaoh died a believer, free of sins. As he said:

“Moses was a delight to Pharaoh through the faith Allah granted him as he drowned. He therefore took him in death pure and purified, with no trace of impurity, for He took him at the moment he believed, before he could commit any sins; and Islam wipes out what came before it.”

Yet it is a matter of indisputable religious knowledge among Muslims, Jews, and Christians that Pharaoh was one of the greatest disbelievers in Allah among all creation. Indeed, no account Allah has given in the Quran of a disbeliever mentioned by his own name surpasses the account of Pharaoh; nor has He related greater unbelief, tyranny, or arrogance on the part of any disbeliever than He has related of Pharaoh.

He has also stated that Pharaoh and his people will enter the severest punishment. For the expression “the people of Pharaoh” is like the expressions “the family of Abraham,” “the family of Lot,” “the family of David,” and “the family of Abū Awfā”: by unanimous agreement, the person to whom the group is ascribed is himself included.

Thus, when they take the greatest human enemy of Allah—or one of His greatest enemies—and declare him correct and justified in the very thing for which Allah declared him an unbeliever, it becomes clear that what they have said is greater unbelief than that of the Jews and Christians. What, then, of their other doctrines?

---

## Page 508

The early generations of the Muslim community and its imams agreed that the Creator, exalted is He, is distinct from His creation: nothing of His creation is within His essence, and nothing of His essence is within His creation.

The early generations and the imams declared the Jahmiyyah unbelievers when they claimed that He is everywhere. Among their objections to this claim was: How could He be inside bellies, latrines, and privies? Exalted is Allah above that! What, then, of those who make Him the very existence of bellies, latrines, privies, impurities, and filth?

The early generations of the Muslim community and its imams agreed that nothing is like Allah, whether in His essence, His attributes, or His actions. Some of the imams said:

“Whoever likens Allah to His creation has disbelieved, and whoever denies what Allah has described Himself with has disbelieved. Neither what Allah has described Himself with nor what His Messenger has described Him with constitutes likening Him to creation.”

How do those who liken Allah to His creation and ascribe a body to Him compare with these people? The furthest the former go in their unbelief is to make Him like created beings. Yet they say that He is eternal and that created beings came into existence. These people, however, have made Him identical with created beings, making Him the very created bodies themselves. They have attributed to Him all the imperfections and defects attributed to every disbeliever, every wicked person, every devil, every predatory beast, and every snake.

Exalted is Allah above their falsehood and misguidance! Glorified and exalted is He, far above what they say. Allah Most High exacts retribution from them for Himself, His religion, His Book, His Messenger, and His believing servants.

---

## Page 509

These people say that the Christians disbelieved only because they confined this identification to Christ, saying:

“Allah is the Messiah.”

Everything the Christians say about Christ, these people say about Allah. The Christians’ unbelief is but one part of theirs. When the aforementioned book was read to the most eminent of their later figures, someone said to him, “This book contradicts the Quran.” He replied, “The entire Quran is polytheism. Monotheism is found only in these words of ours.” He meant that the Quran distinguishes between the Lord and the servant, whereas, for them, the reality of monotheism is that the Lord is the servant.

The questioner then asked him, “What difference, then, is there between my wife and my daughter?” He replied, “There is no difference. But these veiled people said, ‘It is forbidden,’ so we said, ‘It is forbidden for you.’”

To describe their doctrine as unbelief does not adequately convey its nature, for unbelief is a category encompassing forms of varying degrees. Indeed, every disbeliever’s unbelief is but one part of theirs. Thus, when their leader was told, “You are a Nusayrī,” he replied, “Nusayr is a part of me.”

ʿAbd Allah ibn al-Mubārak used to say, “We can recount the statements of the Jews and Christians, but we cannot recount the statements of the Jahmiyyah.” These people are worse than those Jahmiyyah: the furthest the latter went was to say that Allah is everywhere, whereas these people say that He is the very existence of every place. They do not recognize two existing entities, one indwelling and the other the locus of that indwelling.

Thus they said, “Adam’s relation to Allah is like that of the pupil to the eye.” Muslims, Jews, and Christians know, as a necessarily established teaching of the religion of the messengers, that whoever says of any human being that he is a part of Allah is a disbeliever in every faith, for the Christians did not say this

---

## Page 510

—though their doctrine is among the gravest forms of unbelief—no one said that created beings themselves are part of the Creator, or that the Creator is creation, or that the Real, who is beyond all likeness, is creation, which is subject to likeness.

Likewise, his statement, “If the polytheists were to abandon idol worship, they would be ignorant of the Real in proportion to how much of it they abandoned,” constitutes unbelief that is indisputably recognized as such in all faiths. For the adherents of all faiths agree that all the messengers were forbidden to worship idols and declared those who did so disbelievers, and that a believer cannot truly be a believer until he renounces the worship of idols and everything worshipped besides Allah. As Allah Most High says:

“You have an excellent example in Abraham and those with him, when they said to their people, ‘We disavow you and whatever you worship besides Allah. We reject you, and enmity and hatred have arisen between us and you forever, until you believe in Allah alone.’”

And Allah’s intimate friend said:

“Have you considered what you have been worshipping—you and your forefathers? They are enemies to me, except the Lord of all worlds.”

And Allah’s intimate friend said “to his father and his people, ‘I disavow whatever you worship, except the One who created me, for He will guide me.’”

And Allah’s intimate friend—the leader of those devoted to pure monotheism, in whose descendants Allah placed prophethood and Scripture—made the declaration for which the adherents of all faiths agree to venerate him:

“O my people, I disavow whatever you associate with Allah. I have turned my face towards the One who created the heavens and the earth, devoted to pure monotheism, and I am not one of the polytheists.”

This is too widely established and too evident among the adherents of the faiths—the Jews and Christians, let alone the Muslims—to require support from any particular text. Thus, whoever says, “If those who worship idols were to abandon them, they would be ignorant of the Real in proportion to how many of these idols they abandoned,” is a greater disbeliever than

---

## Page 511

the Jews and Christians. Whoever does not regard such people as disbelievers is himself a greater disbeliever than the Jews and Christians, for the Jews and Christians regard idol worshippers as disbelievers. What, then, of someone who holds that whoever abandons idol worship becomes ignorant of the Real in proportion to how much of it he abandons?

Moreover, he says:

“For the knowledgeable person knows whom he has worshipped, and in what form He appeared to receive that worship. He knows that differentiation and multiplicity are like the limbs of a perceptible form and the immaterial faculties of a spiritual form. Thus, whatever the object of worship, he has worshipped none but Allah.”

Indeed, this is worse unbelief than that of idol worshippers, for they took their idols as intercessors and intermediaries, as they said:

“We worship them only so that they may bring us nearer to Allah.”

And Allah Most High says:

“Or have they taken intercessors besides Allah? Say, ‘Even though they possess nothing and have no understanding?’”

They acknowledged that Allah was the Creator of the heavens and the earth and the Creator of the idols, as Allah Most High says:

“If you ask them who created the heavens and the earth, they will surely say, ‘Allah.’”

And Allah Most High says:

“Most of them do not believe in Allah without also associating others with Him.”

Ibn ‘Abbās said: “You ask them who created the heavens and the earth, and they say, ‘Allah,’ yet they worship others besides Him.” In their pilgrimage invocation, they would say: “Here I am at Your service! You have no partner except a partner who belongs to You: You own him and all that he owns.”

For this reason, Allah Most High says:

“He sets forth for you an example drawn from yourselves: do you have, among those whom your right hands possess, any partners in what We have provided for you, so that you are equal in it and fear them as you fear one another?”

These people are greater in unbelief because they regard the idol worshipper as worshipping Allah, not anyone besides Him, and regard the idols as part of Allah, just as a person’s limbs are part of that person

---

## Page 512

and as the faculties of the soul are part of the soul; whereas idol worshippers acknowledged that their idols were other than Him and were created. These people are also greater in unbelief in that the Arab idol worshippers acknowledged that the heavens and the earth had a Lord distinct from them who created them. These people, however, hold that the heavens, the earth, and all other created things have no Lord distinct from them; rather, the creation itself is the Creator. For this reason, he declared that the people of ‘Ād and other disbelievers were on a straight path, placed them at the very heart of nearness to Allah, and claimed that the inhabitants of Hell enjoy themselves there just as the inhabitants of Paradise enjoy themselves in Paradise.

It is an indisputably established teaching of Islam that the peoples of ‘Ād and Thamūd, Pharaoh and his people, and all the other disbelievers whose stories Allah has recounted are enemies of Allah; that they will be punished in the Hereafter; and that Allah has cursed them and is angry with them. Whoever praises them and counts them among those brought near to Allah and those who enjoy bliss is, in this respect, greater in unbelief than the Jews and Christians.

This fatwa cannot accommodate a detailed exposition of these people’s statements and an explanation of their unbelief and heresy. They are of the same kind as the esotericist Qarmatians and the Ismā‘īlīs, who were greater in unbelief than the Jews and Christians. Their doctrine entails disbelief in all the revealed Books and Messengers. As Shaykh Ibrāhīm al-Ja‘barī said after meeting Ibn ‘Arabī—the author of this book:

“I found him to be a filthy shaykh who denies every Book Allah has revealed and every prophet Allah has sent.”

---

## Page 513

And when the jurist Abū Muhammad ibn ‘Abd al-Salām arrived in Cairo and was asked about him, he said:

“He is an evil shaykh, an accursed liar who holds that the world has no beginning and regards no sexual relations as forbidden.”

His statement, “He holds that the world has no beginning,” reflected the man’s actual position. This is a well-known form of unbelief, and the jurist Abū Muhammad declared him a disbeliever on that account. His teaching that the world is Allah, and that the world is Allah’s form and His very identity, had not yet come to light. This is greater unbelief than that of those who maintain that the world has no beginning while affirming a Necessary Existent and holding that contingent existence emanated from Him.

Shaykhs who had met him in person said that he was a liar and a fabricator. His books, such as al-Futūhāt al-Makkiyya (The Meccan Revelations) and others, contain lies that no discerning person could fail to recognize. Yet he is closer to Islam than Ibn Sab‘īn, al-Qūnawī, al-Tilimsānī, and others among his followers. If the one who is closer holds such unbelief—which is greater than the unbelief of the Jews and Christians—what of those who are further from Islam? I have not described even a tenth of the unbelief they express.

Nevertheless, their true nature was unclear to those unfamiliar with their condition, just as the true nature of the esotericist Qarmatians was unclear when they claimed to be Fatimids and professed allegiance to Shiism. Their followers thus became inclined toward them, unaware of their concealed unbelief. For this reason, anyone who inclined toward them was one of two kinds: either a hypocritical heretic or an ignorant, misguided man.

The same applies to these proponents of the identity of Creator and creation: their leading figures are leaders of unbelief who must be put to death, and repentance is not accepted from

---

## Page 514

a hypocritical heretic, or an ignorant, misguided man.
