he made clear that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, had not singled him out for these reports, but had related them to everyone. He said:

“The most knowledgeable among us was the one who remembered best.”

Further evidence of this is the report in the Sunan collections:

“In the year of the conquest, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, had declared the lives of several people forfeit, including ‘Abd Allah ibn Abī Sarh. ‘Uthmān brought him to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, so that he could pledge allegiance to him. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, hesitated for a while before accepting his pledge, then said, ‘Was there no discerning man among you who, seeing me refrain from accepting this man’s pledge, would strike off his head?’

“A man of the Ansār said, ‘O Messenger of Allah, why did you not signal to me?’

“He replied, ‘It is not fitting for a prophet to make a treacherous signal with his eyes.’”

This and similar reports show that the Prophet’s outward conduct and inward disposition, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, were consistent: he did not present people with something contrary to what he concealed within, as the heretics among the philosophizers, the Qarmatians, the misguided ascetics, and others like them claim.

Seventh: He said:

“Among us are those who know yet do not say such things, and this is the loftiest statement. Rather, knowledge and silence bestowed this upon him; inability did not. Such a person possesses the highest knowledge of Allah. This knowledge belongs only to the Seal of the Messengers and the Seal of Allah’s close servants. None of Allah’s close servants or messengers sees it except through the lamp-niche of the final Messenger, and none of Allah’s close servants sees it except through the lamp-niche of the final close servant. Indeed, whenever the messengers see it, they see it only through the lamp-niche of the Seal of Allah’s close servants. For messengership and prophethood—I mean prophethood and messengership that bring legislation—come to an end, whereas closeness to Allah never ends. Thus, in their capacity as Allah’s close servants, the messengers see what we have mentioned only through the lamp-niche of the Seal of Allah’s close servants. What, then, of those close servants who are beneath them? Even though the Seal of Allah’s close servants is a follower

---

## Page 602

of the legislation brought by the Seal of the Messengers as regards legal rulings, this does not detract from his station or contradict our position, for in one respect he is lower, just as in another he is higher”—up to his words: “And when the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, likened prophethood to a wall made of bricks.”

These words contain forms of heresy, disbelief, and disparagement of the prophets and messengers that neither the Jews nor the Christians would utter.

How closely his words here resemble the saying he mentioned, “The roof fell down upon them from beneath them,” which he described as “neither reason nor Quran.” Likewise, his assertion here—that the prophets and messengers acquire knowledge from the Seal of Allah’s close servants, who comes after them—contradicts reason, for those who come earlier do not acquire knowledge from those who come later.

It also contradicts the revealed law, for it is necessarily known in the religion of Islam that the prophets and messengers are superior to Allah’s close servants who are neither prophets nor messengers.

He may claim that the knowledge he possesses is the highest knowledge. This is the doctrine of the oneness of existence: that the existence of the Creator is the very existence of creation. In reality, it amounts to negating and denying the Maker—the very doctrine that Pharaoh openly professed. It was not enough for him to claim that this was true; he went on to claim that it was the highest knowledge. Nor was that enough for him; he further claimed that the messengers perceive it only through the lamp-niche of the Seal of Allah’s close servants. Thus, he made the Seal of Allah’s close servants more knowledgeable about Allah than all the prophets and messengers, and made them perceive knowledge of Allah through his lamp-niche.

He then began to explain this, saying:

“For messengership and prophethood—I mean prophethood and messengership that bring legislation—

---

## Page 603

come to an end, whereas closeness to Allah never comes to an end. Thus the messengers, in their capacity as Allah’s close servants, perceive what we have mentioned only through the lamp-niche of the Seal of Allah’s close servants. What, then, of those close servants who are neither prophets nor messengers?”

This is because they could not posit a prophet and messenger after the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, for that would be manifest disbelief. They therefore claimed that only legislative prophethood and messengership come to an end. They mean that prophethood and messengership of realization—which, according to them, constitute closeness to Allah—have not come to an end. According to them, this closeness to Allah is superior to prophethood and messengership. Hence Ibn ʿArabī said elsewhere:

“The station of prophethood lies between:
just above the messenger, below Allah’s close servant.”

He also said in al-Fusūs (The Bezels), in the chapter concerning Uzayr:

“If you hear someone among the people of Allah Most High say—or it is reported to you that he said—‘Closeness to Allah is higher than prophethood,’ the speaker means only what we have mentioned. Or he may say, ‘Allah’s close servant is above the prophet and messenger.’ By this he means these qualities within a single person: the messenger, peace be upon him, is more complete in his capacity as Allah’s close servant than in his capacity as a prophet and messenger. He does not mean that a close servant who follows him is higher than he is, for a follower can never equal the one he follows in that in which he follows him. If he equalled him, he would no longer be his follower.”

When this point is examined closely, they say that the Prophet’s closeness to Allah is above his prophethood, and that his prophethood is above his messengership, because through his closeness to Allah he receives from Allah. They then claim for themselves a closeness to Allah like his, and hold that the closeness of the Seal of Allah’s close servants is greater than his, and that the messenger’s closeness to Allah is subordinate to that of the Seal of Allah’s close servants whom they have posited.

---

## Page 604

This discourse has several aspects which we have explained elsewhere:

Among them is that the claim that a Seal of Allah’s close servants exists in the manner they describe is false and has no basis. No well-known figure before them mentioned this except Abū ‘Abd Allāh Muhammad ibn ‘Alī al-Tirmidhī al-Hakīm in his book Khatm al-Wilāya (The Seal of Closeness to Allah). In this book, he included erroneous statements contrary to the Quran, the Sunnah, and scholarly consensus. Although he—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—possessed merit and knowledge, and his writings contain commendable examples of sound, acceptable discourse and beneficial insights, they also contain errors that must be rejected. Among the most objectionable are those he mentioned in Khatm al-Wilāya, such as his claim that among later generations there would be someone whose rank before Allah was higher than that of Abū Bakr, ‘Umar, and others.

Yet he contradicted himself elsewhere. When he reported that some people held that a close servant of Allah lives apart from other people, he rejected this, citing Abū Bakr and ‘Umar as evidence. He said, “This would imply that he is superior to Abū Bakr and ‘Umar,” and rejected that conclusion.

Another error is that he included statements in his book suggesting that abandoning outward acts of worship—even prescribed voluntary acts—is better for someone who has attained perfection and performs acts of the heart. This, too, is an error according to the masters of the spiritual path. For the most perfect of all creation is the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and the best guidance is the guidance of Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. Until his death, he continued to observe whatever regular devotions and voluntary bodily acts of worship he was able to perform.

---

## Page 605

(Another aspect)

is his claim concerning a Seal of Allah’s close servants who will appear at the end of time, whom he considers superior to and ranks above all the close servants who preceded him, and whose standing among them will be like that of the Seal of the Prophets among the prophets. This is manifest misguidance. The best of Allah’s close servants in this nation are Abū Bakr, ‘Umar, ‘Uthmān, ‘Alī, and others like them among the earliest pioneers of the Emigrants and Helpers, as established by well-known scriptural texts.

The best generation is that of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, as stated in the authentic hadith:

“The best of generations is my generation, among whom I was sent, then those who follow them, then those who follow them.”

Al-Tirmidhī and others relate that he said concerning Abū Bakr and ‘Umar:

“These two are the leaders of the middle-aged men among the people of Paradise, from the earliest and the latest generations, except the prophets and messengers.”

Al-Tirmidhī said, “It is a good hadith.”

In Sahīh al-Bukhārī, it is related that ‘Alī, may Allah be pleased with him, was asked by his son, “Father, who is the best of people after the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace?” He replied, “My son, Abū Bakr.” His son asked, “Then who?” He replied, “Then ‘Umar.”

More than eighty people related from him that he said, “The best of this nation after its Prophet is Abū Bakr, then ‘Umar.”

This is an extensive subject. Allah Most High says:

“They will be with those upon whom Allah has bestowed His favour: the prophets, the utterly truthful, the martyrs, and the righteous.”

These four are the ranks of Allah’s servants: the highest are the prophets, then the utterly truthful, then the martyrs, then the righteous. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, forbade any of us to regard himself as superior to Jonah son of Mattā—even though Allah says, “And do not be like the companion of the fish,” and, “While he was blameworthy”—thereby indicating that it is all the more fitting that no one regard himself as superior to the other prophets. For in Sahīh al-Bukhārī, it is related from Ibn

---

## Page 606

Mas‘ūd that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Let none of you say, ‘I am better than Jonah son of Mattā.’”

Sahīh al-Bukhārī also relates from him that the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“It is not fitting for a servant to be better than Jonah son of Mattā.”

In another wording:

“to say, ‘I am better than Jonah son of Mattā.’”

Al-Bukhārī also relates from Abū Hurayrah that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Whoever says, ‘I am better than Jonah son of Mattā,’ has lied.”

The two Sahīhs relate from Abū Hurayrah that he—meaning the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“It is not fitting for a servant to say, ‘I am better than Jonah son of Mattā.’”

The two Sahīhs also relate from Ibn ‘Abbās that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said—in one wording, relating from his Lord:

“It is not fitting for a servant to say, ‘I am better than Jonah son of Mattā.’”

This contains a general prohibition.

As for the report transmitted by some people that he said:

“Do not regard me as superior to Jonah son of Mattā,”

which they interpret as equating the state of the one who made the heavenly ascent with that of the companion of the fish, both the report and its interpretation are false. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Be firm, Uhud! There is none upon you but a prophet, an utterly truthful man, or a martyr.”

Abū Bakr is the best of the utterly truthful.

The expression “Seal of Allah’s close servants” is not found in the words of any of this nation’s early generations or its leading scholars, nor is it mentioned in Allah’s Book or the Sunnah of His Messenger. What this expression implies is that he is the last pious believer, for Allah says:

“Indeed, Allah’s close servants will have no fear, nor will they grieve”—and the rest of the verse.

“Everyone who is a pious believer is a close servant of Allah.”

They are of two ranks: the foremost, who are brought near, and the companions of the right, who follow a middle course, as Allah Most High has classified them in the surahs Fātir, al-Wāqi‘ah, al-Insān, and al-Mutaffifīn.

---

## Page 607

Sahīh al-Bukhārī relates from Abū Hurayrah that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Allah Most High says: ‘Whoever shows enmity towards a close servant of Mine has openly challenged Me to war. Nothing by which My servant draws near to Me compares with fulfilling what I have made obligatory upon him. My servant continues to draw near to Me through voluntary acts of worship until I love him. When I love him, I am his hearing with which he hears, his sight with which he sees, his hand with which he grasps, and his foot with which he walks. I have never hesitated over anything I am to do as I hesitate over taking the soul of My believing servant: he dislikes death, and I dislike causing him distress, yet he must undergo it.’”

Those who draw near to Allah through obligatory duties are the righteous who follow a middle course, the companions of the right. Those who draw near to Him through the voluntary acts He loves, after fulfilling their obligatory duties, are the foremost, who are brought near. Voluntary acts come only after obligatory duties.

Abū Bakr al-Siddīq said in his final counsel to ‘Umar ibn al-Khattāb:

“Know that Allah has a right over you at night which He will not accept during the day, and a right over you during the day which He will not accept at night. He will not accept a voluntary act until you fulfil the obligatory duty.”

The proponents of union claim that drawing near through voluntary acts entails the Real Himself being identical with the servant’s limbs, and that drawing near through obligatory duties entails the Real being identical with the servant’s entire existence. This is false in many respects; indeed, it is outright disbelief, as we have explained elsewhere.

If the Seal of Allah’s close servants is the last pious believer in this world, then that man is neither the best nor the most perfect of Allah’s close servants. Rather, the best and most perfect among them are their predecessors, who are more closely associated with the best of the messengers than others are. The more closely a servant is associated with the Messenger, learns from him, and conforms to his example, the better he is. For no one becomes a close servant of Allah except by following the Messenger inwardly and outwardly. The degree to which he follows the Messenger determines his standing as a close servant of Allah.

---

## Page 608

The close servants of Allah do include men who receive divine inspiration, as established in the two Sahīh collections, where the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Among the nations before you there were men who received divine inspiration. If there is anyone like that in my community, it is ‘Umar.”

This hadith indicates that ‘Umar ranks first among those who receive divine inspiration in this community. Yet Abū Bakr is superior to him, for he is the consummately truthful one. A person who receives divine inspiration—even though he is inspired and inwardly addressed from Allah Most High—must test this against the Quran and Sunnah, for he is not infallible. As Abū al-Hasan al-Shādhilī said:

“We have been guaranteed protection from error in what the Quran and Sunnah have brought, but we have not been guaranteed protection from error in spiritual unveilings and inspiration.”

For this reason, ‘Umar ibn al-Khattāb would always yield to the Book of Allah. Abū Bakr al-Siddīq would explain to him matters that contradicted what occurred to him, as he did at al-Hudaybiyyah, on the day the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, died, when fighting those who withheld zakat, and on other occasions.

‘Umar ibn al-Khattāb would consult the Companions: sometimes he accepted their judgment, and sometimes they accepted his. At times he would express an opinion, and a Muslim woman would refute it and explain the truth to him; he would then accept her correction and abandon his opinion, as happened when he set a limit on the marriage dowry. At other times he would hold an opinion, then be reminded of a hadith from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, whereupon he would act upon it and abandon his opinion. On numerous matters, he learned aspects of the Sunnah from people of lesser standing than himself. He would express an opinion and be told, “You are right,” yet reply, “By Allah, ‘Umar does not know whether he has reached the truth or missed it.”

If this was the case with the foremost of those who receive divine inspiration, then everyone whose heart speaks to him from his Lord, until the Day of Resurrection, is below ‘Umar. None of them is infallible; rather, all of them are liable to error, even though

---

## Page 609

some people claim that a close servant of Allah is protected, in a manner comparable to the infallibility established for the prophets—a view to which al-Hakīm al-Tirmidhī has alluded. This is false and contrary to the Sunnah and scholarly consensus.

For this reason, Muslims agree that anyone’s statements may be accepted or rejected except those of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, although people differ in their guidance, light, and correctness. Thus, the consummately truthful person is superior to the person who receives divine inspiration: the consummately truthful person draws from the light of prophethood and therefore receives only what is free from error and safeguarded. By contrast, someone who receives divine inspiration may be right or wrong, and the Quran and Sunnah distinguish his correct insights from his errors. Hence, all the close servants of Allah depend upon the Quran and Sunnah. They must weigh all their affairs against the teachings transmitted from the Messenger. Whatever agrees with those teachings is true, and whatever contradicts them is false, even if they have earnestly striven to arrive at the truth in the matter. Allah Most High rewards them for their efforts and forgives their errors.

It is well known that the earliest forerunners were more fully guided and followed the transmitted prophetic teachings more closely; they therefore possessed greater faith and piety. The last of Allah’s close servants cannot attain what they attained.

As for the reported hadith:

“My community is like rain: one cannot tell whether its beginning is better or its end.”

Its chain of transmission has been questioned. Even assuming that it is authentic, it means only that, toward the end of the community’s history, there will be people who come close to its earliest members, so that some people will be unsure which group is better—just as some people cannot distinguish between the two ends of a garment—even though it is certain that the earlier group is better than the later one. This is why he said:

“One cannot tell.”

It is well known that this negation does not apply to everyone in the community, for it must be known which of the two groups is superior.

---

## Page 610

Moreover, the title “Seal of Allah’s close servants” has come to denote an imaginary rank with no reality. Various groups have claimed it for themselves or their shaykhs, and more than one person has laid claim to it. Yet none has claimed it except those whose words contain falsehoods that neither the Jews nor the Christians have uttered. Thus, the author of al-Fusūs claimed it, followed by the writer on letters, a shaykh among their followers who lived in Damascus, and another who claimed that he was the Mahdī who would marry his daughter to Jesus son of Mary, and that he was the Seal of Allah’s close servants. These people and others like them claim things that belong to Allah alone, just as one of them may claim for himself or his shaykh what the Christians have claimed for the Messiah.

The author of al-Fusūs and others like him then based their position on the premise that a close servant of Allah receives from Allah without an intermediary, whereas a prophet receives through the intermediary of an angel. For this reason, they regard the Seal of Allah’s close servants as superior in this respect. This is false and a lie. A close servant of Allah receives from Allah only through the Messenger sent to him. If he is someone who receives divine inspiration and something has been imparted to him, he must weigh it against the Book and Sunnah brought by the Messenger.

Allah speaks to His servants in three ways: from behind a veil, as He spoke to Moses; by sending a messenger, as He sent angels to the prophets; and through inspiration, in which a close servant of Allah has a share. As for the first two forms, they are exclusive to the prophets. Thus, Allah’s close servants, for whom the messengers have established the proof, receive knowledge of religion only through the mediation of Allah’s messengers sent to them—even if that mediation consists only in submitting it to what the Messenger brought

---

## Page 611

and they will never attain the rank of a prophet or messenger in what they receive from Allah. How, then, can they receive from Allah without an intermediary, and how can this manner of receiving be superior, when they cannot attain the station of Moses, to whom Allah spoke, nor have angels descend upon them as they descended upon the prophets? This is the belief of Muslims, Jews, and Christians.

As for these Jahmīs who profess the unity of existence, they built upon their corrupt premise that Allah is the absolute existence shared by all that exists. Thus, whatever thoughts occur in their hearts—even if they are Satan’s whisperings—they claim to have received from Allah without an intermediary, and they claim that they are spoken to just as Moses son of ʿImrān was spoken to. Some of them even claim that their state is superior to that of Moses son of ʿImrān, because Moses heard the address from the tree, whereas they—as they claim—hear it from a living, speaking being. Thus, the author of al-Fusūs is reported to have said:

“All speech in existence is His speech;
To us, its prose and verse are alike.”

What helped them reach this conclusion was their adherence to the doctrines of the Jahmīs and their followers, who claim that Allah’s speaking to Moses was merely a form of inspiration, and that a servant may see Allah in this world once the impediment is removed from his sight. According to them, no veil obstructing vision exists separately from the servant; rather, the veil is attached to him. Once it is lifted, he beholds the Truth. Yet they behold only their mental representation of absolute existence, which has no reality except in their minds, or of created existence. Thus the Lord whom they behold, in their view—who

---

## Page 612

addresses them, as they claim—exists only in their minds, or has no existence apart from that of created beings. This amounts to denying the Lord Most High, His Books, and His messengers. Religious innovations are a gateway to unbelief and hypocrisy, just as Shi‘ism is a gateway to Rāfidism, and Rāfidism is a gateway to Qarmatian doctrine and denial of God. Thus, speculative theology containing Jahmī elements is a gateway to Jahmism, and Jahmism is a gateway to heresy and denial of God.

It is authentically reported in Sahīh Muslim that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Know that none of you will see his Lord until he dies.”

For this reason, the early generations of the Muslim community and its leading scholars agreed that Allah will be seen in the Hereafter, and that no one sees Him with his eyes in this world.

Aisha and Ibn ‘Abbās expressed well-known views concerning whether the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, saw his Lord. Aisha denied that he saw Him, whereas it is authentically reported from Ibn ‘Abbās in Sahīh Muslim that he said:

“Muhammad saw his Lord with his heart twice.”

Ahmad likewise reported from Abū Dharr and others the affirmation that the Prophet saw Him with his heart. This explicitly reported position of Ibn ‘Abbās, Abū Dharr, and others is also the position explicitly reported from Ahmad and other leading scholars of the Sunnah. No affirmation of seeing Allah with the eyes in this world has been authentically established from any of them, just as no denial of seeing Him in the Hereafter has been authentically established from any of them.

Both positions, however, are held by groups among the Jahmīs. Jahmī speculative theologians deny the vision, while some Jahmī Sufis—such as those who profess the unity of existence—and a group of others affirm it. Those who profess the unity of existence combine denial and affirmation, as Ibn Sab‘īn says:

“The very thing you see is an essence not seen, and an essence not seen is the very thing you see.”

They make similar statements, for their doctrine

---

## Page 613

necessarily entails combining two contradictory propositions. They say of all created beings what the Christians say of Christ, and thus their views differ just as Christian views concerning Christ differ.

Among their claims is that the Seal of the saints is superior to the Seal of the prophets in certain respects. Neither Abū ‘Abd Allāh al-Hakīm al-Tirmidhī nor any other well-known spiritual master said this. Indeed, his stature was too great and his faith too strong for him to fabricate such blatant unbelief. But he erred by a handspan, and they built upon his error until it became unbelief.

Still worse is their claim that the saints and messengers, in respect of their sainthood, follow the Seal of the saints and draw their light from his lamp-niche. This is false according to both reason and religion: those who come earlier do not receive from those who come later, and the messengers do not receive from others.

Worse still, he made them his followers in knowledge of Allah, the noblest of their sciences. More blatant still, he equated knowledge of Allah with the doctrine of those who profess the unity of existence, who say that the existence of created beings is identical to the existence of the Creator.

Let the believer reflect upon this vile unbelief, step by step.

He cites the incident involving ‘Umar and that of pollinating date palms as evidence that someone other than the Prophet may be superior to him. Would any Muslim say that ‘Umar was superior to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, because of his opinion concerning the captives? Or that farmers skilled in pollinating date palms are superior to the prophets in that respect?

Yet he was not content with this, but went on to say:

“The perfect man need not have preeminence in every science and every rank. Men look only to preeminence in the rank of knowledge of Allah; that is what they seek.”

---

## Page 614

Thus he claimed to know Allah better than the Seal of the prophets, and that his own preeminence lay in knowledge of Allah, whereas the Seal of the prophets surpassed him only in prescribing religious law. This is among the gravest forms of unbelief into which extremists among philosophers, Sufis, and speculative theologians fall. They claim to be more accomplished than the messengers in matters of knowledge, such as knowledge of Allah and the like, and that the messengers surpassed them only in prescribing general religious laws intended to put people’s worldly affairs in order.

They sometimes say that revealed laws are rules of justice instituted for worldly welfare. As for knowledge, realities, and lofty ranks in this world and the Hereafter, they consider themselves and their paths superior to the prophets and their paths.

It is necessarily known from the religion of the Muslims that this is among the gravest forms of unbelief and misguidance. This was among the causes of their denial of the realities proclaimed by the messengers concerning faith in Allah and the Last Day, and of their claim that what these people say on this subject is the truth. In dealing with the messengers’ reports, they sometimes reject them as false, sometimes distort them, sometimes leave their meanings to Allah, and sometimes claim that the messengers lied for the benefit of the public.

Nevertheless, most of those who make these claims regard the prophets and messengers as superior to themselves, except for the extremists among them, as mentioned above. These extremists are among the worst people in their statements and beliefs. There was among us a shaykh who was one of the most ignorant of people, yet a group of non-Arabs revered him, and he was said to be the Seal of the saints. He claimed that he explained knowledge in two ways, whereas the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, had explained it in only one, and that he himself was more perfect than the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.

---

## Page 615

and he learned this from the author of al-Fusūs. Many people like him exist in these times. The misguidance of the philosophizers, the Sufis, and the speculative theologians stems from their acceptance of such errors. This is not the place for a lengthy exposition of this misguidance; the purpose is merely to point out that the author of al-Fusūs and others like him expressed these same views.

The unbelief of someone who considers himself superior to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace—as the author of al-Fusūs stated—is evident. Some of these people, however, do not hold that view, but believe that they have a path to Allah other than following the Messenger. They consider themselves entitled to follow that path even if it conflicts with the Messenger’s revealed law, and they invoke the account of Moses and al-Khidr as evidence.

That account provides no support for their position, for two reasons.

First: Moses was not sent to al-Khidr, nor was al-Khidr obliged to follow Moses, for Moses was sent to the Children of Israel. Thus, the authentic hadith relates:

“When Moses greeted al-Khidr, al-Khidr said, ‘How is this greeting known in your land?’

“He replied, ‘I am Moses.’

“Al-Khidr asked, ‘Moses of the Children of Israel?’

“He replied, ‘Yes.’

“Al-Khidr said, ‘You possess knowledge from Allah which Allah has taught you and which I do not know; and I possess knowledge from Allah which He has taught me and which you do not know.’”

For this reason, our Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“We have been favored over other people in five respects: our rows have been made like the rows of the angels; the earth has been made for me a place of prayer and a means of purification, so wherever a man finds himself when prayer becomes due, he has his place of prayer and his means of purification at hand; war booty has been made lawful for me, though it was not lawful for anyone before me; I have been granted intercession; and a prophet used to be sent specifically to his own people, whereas I have been sent to

---

## Page 616

all people.” He also said:

“I have been granted five things that no one before me was granted: I have been granted victory through fear instilled in the enemy a month’s journey away; the earth has been made for me a place of prayer and a means of purification; war booty has been made lawful for me, though it was not lawful for anyone before me; I have been granted intercession; and a prophet used to be sent specifically to his own people, whereas I have been sent to all people.”

Allah Most High said:

“We have sent you only as a bearer of good tidings and a warner to all mankind.”

And He Most High said:

“Say, ‘O mankind, I am the Messenger of Allah to you all,’”

and the verse continues.

Thus Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is Allah’s Messenger to all humankind and jinn: humans and jinn, Arabs and non-Arabs, kings and ascetics, Allah’s allies among them and those who are not. No one may depart from following him, inwardly or outwardly, or from following the Book and Sunnah that he brought, in any matter, small or great, whether concerning knowledge or deeds. No one may say to him what al-Khidr said to Moses, for Moses was not sent to al-Khidr.

Second: The account of al-Khidr contains no violation of the revealed law. Rather, the things he did are permissible under the revealed law when a servant knows their grounds, as al-Khidr did. Hence, when he explained those grounds to Moses, Moses agreed with him. Had he acted contrary to Moses’ revealed law, Moses would never have agreed with him. We have discussed this at length elsewhere.

Making a hole in the boat illustrates that a person may preserve legally protected property for its owner by destroying part of it, for that is better than losing it altogether. Similarly, during the Prophet’s lifetime, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, a shepherd was permitted to slaughter a sheep that he feared would die.

The account of the boy illustrates the permissibility of killing a child who attacks. Hence Ibn ‘Abbās said to Najda:

“As for the boys, if you know about them what al-Khidr knew

---

## Page 617

about that boy, then kill them; otherwise, do not kill them.” As for rebuilding the wall, it illustrates doing good without payment, even when one is in need, if it benefits the descendants of righteous people.

(The eighth point)

He said, “When the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, likened prophethood to a wall”—and so on to the end of his statement. His words imply that knowledge is of two kinds:

(The first)

Knowledge of the revealed law, which he receives from Allah just as the Prophet does. For he said:

“The reason he saw it as two bricks is that he outwardly follows the revealed law of the Seal of the Messengers. This is the position of the silver brick, representing his outward aspect and the rulings in which he follows him. At the same time, he inwardly receives from Allah that in which he is outwardly a follower, for he sees the matter as it truly is, and so he must see it in this way.”

This claim of his—that Allah’s ally inwardly receives from Allah that in which he follows the messengers, as in the relationship between leading scholars and their followers—contains heresy that is plain to anyone who believes in Allah and His messengers. Such a person claims to have been given the like of what Allah’s messengers were given, and says, “Revelation has been given to me,” though nothing has been revealed to him. He places the messengers on the same footing as teachers of medicine, arithmetic, grammar, and other subjects: when a student understands the evidence on which his teacher bases his position, he should agree with him because they share that knowledge, not because the teacher is a messenger and an intermediary from Allah to him, conveying commands and prohibitions.

This disbelief resembles the disbelief of Musaylima the Liar and others like him who claim to share in the Messenger’s mission. His caller to prayer used to say, “I bear witness that Muhammad and Musaylima are messengers of Allah.”

---

## Page 618

(The second)

Knowledge of reality, in which he claims to be superior to the Messenger. As he said:

“This is the position of the golden brick in his inward aspect, for he receives from the source from which the angel receives what he conveys by revelation to the Messenger.”

He thus claims that, in this knowledge represented by the golden brick—knowledge of the inward aspect and of reality—he is superior to the Messenger, because he receives it from the same source from which the angel receives the knowledge he conveys by revelation to the Messenger. The Messenger receives it from the angel, whereas he receives it from above the angel, from the very source from which the angel receives it. This goes beyond the claim of Musaylima the Liar, for Musaylima did not claim to surpass the Messenger in any branch of divine knowledge, whereas this man claims to surpass him in knowledge of Allah.

He then said:

“If you understand what I have indicated, you have attained beneficial knowledge.”

It is well known that this disbelief exceeds the disbelief of the Jews and Christians. The Jews and Christians would not accept placing any believer above Moses and Jesus, whereas this man claims that he and others like him who claim to be the Seal of Allah’s allies are superior to all the messengers and possess greater knowledge of Allah than all the messengers. Even the sensible philosophers would not accept this. Only their extremists and fools say such things—those who are among the furthest removed from reason and religion.

(The ninth point)

His words, “Every prophet, from Adam…”—and so on to the end of the section—imply that all the prophets and messengers receive only from the lamp-niche of the Seal of the Prophets. He thereby seeks to lay the groundwork for his own claim that all the prophets receive only from the lamp-niche of the Seal of Allah’s allies.

---

## Page 619

and both claims are misguidance, for no messenger receives from another unless he has been commanded to follow the other’s law. Such was the case with the prophets of the Children of Israel and the messengers sent among them, who were commanded to follow the Torah, as Allah Most High says:

“Indeed, We sent down the Torah, containing guidance and light”—to the end of the verse.

Abraham did not receive from Moses or Jesus, nor did Noah receive from Abraham. Noah, Abraham, Moses and Jesus did not receive from Muhammad, even though they foretold his coming and believed in him, as Allah Most High says:

“And when Allah took the covenant of the prophets: ‘Whatever I have given you of Scripture and wisdom’”—to the end of the verse.

Ibn ‘Abbās said: “Allah never sent a prophet without taking from him a pledge concerning Muhammad, and taking a pledge from his people that they would surely believe in him and, if he were sent while they were alive, would surely support him.”

(The tenth point)

His statement, “For he exists in his reality, as expressed in his saying, ‘I was a prophet while Adam was between water and clay,’ unlike the other prophets. Likewise, the Seal of Allah’s allies was an ally of Allah while Adam was between water and clay,” is a manifest falsehood that contradicts the consensus of the leading scholars of the religion, even though a group of people of misguidance and heresy say this.

Allah knew all things and decreed them before bringing them into being. They do not exist in their realities until they come into existence, and in this there is no distinction between the prophets and anyone else. The reality of the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, did not exist before he was created, except in the same sense as the reality of anyone else: Allah knew it and decreed it. Yet knowledge of him and his name was far more widespread than that of others, for he was mentioned in writing

---

## Page 620

in the Torah and the Gospel, and even before that, as Imam Ahmad narrated in his Musnad from al-‘Irbād ibn Sāriya that the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“Indeed, I am Allah’s servant, recorded as the Seal of the Prophets while Adam was still lying in his clay. I shall tell you of its beginnings: the supplication of my father Abraham, the glad tidings given by Jesus, and my mother’s vision when she gave birth to me: she saw what appeared to be a light coming from her, illuminating the palaces of Syria.”

There is also the hadith of Maysara al-Fajr:

“I asked, ‘O Messenger of Allah, when were you a prophet?’—in another wording, ‘When were you recorded as a prophet?’ He replied, ‘While Adam was between spirit and body.’”

This is the wording of the hadith. As for the statement:

“I was a prophet while Adam was between water and clay,”

it has no basis. No hadith scholar has narrated it with this wording, and it is false: Adam was not between water and clay, since clay consists of water and earth. Rather, when Allah created Adam’s body, before breathing the spirit into it, He recorded and decreed the prophethood of Muhammad, peace and blessings be upon him. Likewise, it is established in the two Sahīh collections that Ibn Mas‘ūd said: The Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, who is truthful and whose truthfulness is confirmed, told us:

“Each of you is formed in his mother’s womb as a drop of fluid for forty days, then becomes a clot for a similar period, then a lump of flesh for a similar period. Then the angel is sent to him and instructed to record four things. He is told, ‘Record his provision, his deeds, his lifespan, and whether he will be wretched or blessed.’ Then he breathes the spirit into him.”

It has also been narrated that his name was written on the leg of the Throne and the doors of Paradise. But what comparison is there between being recorded and decreed and existing in reality?

The hadiths narrated on this subject are of this kind: for example, that he was a light floating around the Throne, or a star rising in the sky, and the like, as mentioned by

---

## Page 621

Ibn Hamawayh—an associate of Ibn ‘Arabī. Some of these reports were also mentioned by ‘Umar al-Mullā in Wasīlat al-Muta‘abbidīn (The Worshippers’ Means), and by Ibn Sab‘īn and others like them, who transmit reports unanimously judged to be fabricated lies by those versed in hadith. They have narrated hadiths conveying this meaning, all of which are false. Indeed, long ago I met a highly revered shaykh among Ibn Hamawayh’s followers, whom his companions called Sultān al-Aqtāb (Sultan of the Poles). We discussed the book al-Fusūs (The Bezels), which he greatly revered, along with its author. When I showed him some of what it contained, he was appalled and began citing reports of this kind. I explained to him that all of them were false.

(Eleventh)

His statement, “The Seal of the Saints was a saint while Adam was between water and clay”—up to his words, “The Seal of the Messengers, with regard to his sainthood, stands in the same relation to the Seal of Sainthood as the saints and messengers stand to him”—and through to the end of the passage, repeats the earlier claim that Allah’s Messenger, peace and blessings be upon him, is like all the other prophets and messengers in relation to this purported Seal: he draws from his lamp-niche knowledge of Allah, which is the highest knowledge, namely the unity of existence. He also states that he is “the leader of them all and the master of Adam’s descendants in opening the door of intercession. Thus he specified a particular circumstance, rather than speaking generally”—up to his words, “Thus Muhammad attained supremacy in this particular station.”

He therefore falsely attributed to Allah’s Messenger, peace and blessings be upon him, the statement:

“I am the master of Adam’s descendants in intercession alone.”

He committed heresy and uttered a fabrication by claiming that the Prophet’s supremacy is confined to intercession and does not extend to the other ranks, whereas the fictitious Seal is supreme in knowledge of Allah and in other spiritual stations.

---

## Page 622

Indeed, I used to say:

“If the person addressing us were someone who preferred Abraham, Moses, or Jesus over Muhammad, peace and blessings be upon him, that would be a tremendous calamity that Muslims could not bear. What, then, of someone who prefers a man from Muhammad’s community over Muhammad and all the prophets and messengers in the noblest branch of knowledge, and claims that they draw this knowledge from his lamp-niche?”

This knowledge is the very height of heresy and unbelief. The person who gives him this preference is among the most misguided of Adam’s descendants and the furthest from the straight path, even though he has spoken at length, written numerous works, possesses knowledge of many things, and holds sway over the hearts of groups engaged in philosophy, Sufism, speculative theology, and jurisprudence, as well as ordinary people. In the eyes of those endowed with knowledge and faith, these words are among the most grievously misguided statements. And Allah knows best.

It has become clear that these words contain disbelief, disparagement of the messengers, contempt for them, and belittlement of them—indeed, disbelief in them and in what they brought—in a manner that cannot escape any believer’s notice. One of the foremost learned men told me that he heard Shaykh Ibrāhīm al-Ja‘barī—may Allah have mercy on him—say:

“I saw Ibn ‘Arabī—a filthy shaykh—denying every Book Allah had revealed and every prophet Allah had sent.”

He certainly spoke the truth, but this is only one of the forms of disbelief he mentioned.

Likewise, Abū Muhammad ibn ‘Abd al-Salām’s statement:

“He is an evil, contemptible shaykh, a liar

---

## Page 623

who maintains that the world is eternal and considers no sexual union forbidden”—is true of him, but it covers only some of the forms of disbelief mentioned. For that statement was made before his true position had become clear to him and been firmly ascertained. Otherwise, he does not hold that there is a Lord and a world, as do the theistic philosophers who affirm a Necessary Existent and a contingent world. Rather, in his view, the existence of the world is the existence of Allah. This accords with the position of the naturalist eternalists, who deny the existence of a Maker altogether and acknowledge no necessary existence other than the world itself, as Allah related concerning Pharaoh and his followers.

His position accords with Pharaoh’s, except that Pharaoh did not acknowledge Allah, whereas these people acknowledge Allah but interpret Him as the existence that Pharaoh acknowledged. They are therefore more ignorant and more misguided than Pharaoh. Pharaoh, however, is more deeply steeped in disbelief than they are, for his disbelief contains a degree of obstinacy and arrogance absent from theirs. As the Most High said:

“And they rejected them, while their souls were convinced of them, out of injustice and haughtiness.”

And Moses said to him:

“He said, ‘You have already known that none has sent down these signs except the Lord of the heavens and the earth as evidence.’”

The entire enterprise of the author of al-Fusūs and his followers amounts to demolishing the three foundations of faith. These foundations are belief in Allah, belief in His messengers, and belief in the Last Day.

As for belief in Allah, they claim that His existence is the existence of the world, and that the world has no Maker other than the world itself. As for the Messenger, they claim that they know Allah better than he does, and better than all the messengers. Among them is one who

---

## Page 624

draws knowledge of Allah—which amounts to denial of the Lord and the oneness of existence—from his lamp-niche; and they claim to equal him in receiving knowledge of the religious law from Allah.

As for belief in the Last Day, he said:

“Only He who keeps His promise remains,
and an eye beholds the true threat.
Even if they enter the abode of misery,
they enjoy pleasure there—a distinct kind of bliss.”

A similar statement is reported from one of the misguided people who preceded him: “The Fire becomes a fiery nature for its inhabitants, which they enjoy.” There would then be no fear, nothing to dread, and no punishment, for it would be something pleasurable.

Moreover, regarding commands and prohibitions, he holds that the one who commands and prohibits and the one who is commanded and prohibited are one. Thus, among his opening words in al-Futūhāt al-Makkiyya (The Meccan Revelations), his largest work, were:

“The Lord is real, and the servant is real;
if only I knew: who is bound by obligation?
If you say, ‘The servant,’ that is the Lord;
if you say, ‘The Lord,’ how can He be bound?”

Elsewhere, the wording is “that one is dead”; I saw it in his own handwriting. This rests upon his fundamental premise: in his view, there is no servant and no existence except the existence of the Lord. Who, then, is bound by obligation? According to his premise, He is both the one imposing the obligation and the one bound by it, as they say: “He sent a messenger from Himself to Himself.”

---

## Page 625

And Ibn al-Fārid likewise said in his poem, which he composed according to their doctrine and entitled Nazm al-Sulūk (The Spiritual Path in Verse):

“I was a messenger sent from myself to myself;
and my essence found proof of me through my signs.”

Its substance is the doctrine of the oneness of existence—the doctrine of Ibn Arabī, Ibn Sab‘īn, and others like them—as he said:

“To her I offer my prayers at the station,
and in them I witness that she prayed to me.
Both of us pray and worship, prostrating to me—
his reality, in union, in every prostration.
No one but myself has ever prayed to me;
nor was my prayer for another in performing any unit of prayer.”

He continues until he says:

“I have always been her, and she has always been me;
there is no distinction: rather, my essence loved itself.”

Statements like this are numerous. And Allah knows best.

Our companion, the jurist and Sufi Abū al-Hasan Alī ibn Qurbās, told me that he visited Shaykh Qutb al-Dīn Ibn al-Qastallānī and found him composing a book. He asked, “What is this?” He replied, “This is a refutation of Ibn Sab‘īn, Ibn al-Fārid, Abū al-Hasan al-Jazlī, and al-Afīf al-Tilimsānī.”

He also told me concerning Jamāl al-Dīn Ibn Wāsil and Shams al-Dīn al-Asbahānī that they both used to

---

## Page 626

reject Ibn Arabī’s statements, demonstrate their falsity, and refute him; and that al-Asbahānī saw him with one of Ibn Arabī’s books and said to him:

“If you acquire any of his books, do not come to me”—or words to that effect.

He also related that when Ibn Wāsil mentioned Ibn Arabī’s account of the apple that had been transformed from a houri, with whom he spoke or had intercourse, he said:

“By Allah, besides whom there is no deity, he is lying.”

Indeed, his oath was truthful.

Our companion, the learned and distinguished Abū Bakr ibn Sālār, related to me from Shaykh Taqī al-Dīn Ibn Daqīq al-Īd—the leading shaykh of his time—from Imam Abū Muhammad ibn Abd al-Salām, that when he arrived in Egypt, they asked him about Ibn Arabī. He replied:

“An evil shaykh, a reprehensible liar, who professes the eternity of the world and considers no sexual union unlawful.”

Taqī al-Dīn used to say, “He has a vivid imagination.” More than one Egyptian jurist who had heard Ibn Daqīq al-Īd’s words related this to me.

Ibn Buhayr related to me from Rashīd al-Dīn Saīd and others that he said:

“He regarded lying as permissible. That is the best that can be said of him.”

The learned shaykh and man of spiritual insight, Kamāl al-Dīn al-Marāghī, the leading shaykh of his time, related to me that when he arrived and heard these people’s statements concerning divine oneness, he said:

“I read some of their writings to al-Afīf al-Tilimsānī and found them contrary to the Book and the Sunnah. When I mentioned this to him, he said, ‘The Quran contains no affirmation of divine oneness; rather, the entire Quran consists of associating partners with Allah. Whoever follows the Quran will never attain divine oneness.’ I said to him, ‘What distinction do you make between a wife, an unrelated woman, and a sister? Are they all one and the same?’ He replied:

---

## Page 627

‘We make no distinction between them. It is only these veiled people who believe such things to be unlawful. So we say that they are unlawful for them according to their own beliefs; as for us, nothing is unlawful.’”

Kamāl al-Dīn al-Marāghī related to me his discussion of this doctrine with al-Tilimsānī, saying:

“I was studying these writings under him, for they had spoken of him to us with great reverence, and we were eager to understand Fusūs al-Hikam (The Bezels of Wisdom). When he began explaining it to me, I would say, ‘This contradicts the Quran and the hadiths.’ He replied, ‘Cast all of that behind the door, and come with a pure heart so that you may receive this divine oneness’—or words to that effect. Then he became afraid that I would circulate what he had said, so he came to me weeping and said, ‘Keep what you heard from me concealed.’”

Kamāl al-Dīn also related to me that he met Shaykh Abū al-Abbās al-Shādhilī, a disciple of Shaykh Abū al-Hasan, who said concerning al-Tilimsānī:

“These men are unbelievers. They believe that the creation is the Creator.”

He continued:

“I had resolved to enter spiritual seclusion under his guidance, so I said, ‘I am not adopting this doctrine from him; I am only learning from him the proper conduct of seclusion.’ He said to me, ‘You are like someone who seeks access to the sultan through a furnace attendant and a refuse collector. If it is the refuse collector who brings him close to the sultan, what standing will he have with the sultan?’”

He also related to us:

“Chief Judge Taqī al-Dīn Ibn Daqīq al-Īd said to me, ‘The Tatars gained control of the eastern lands only because philosophy had become widespread among their inhabitants and because of the weakening of

---

## Page 628

the Shariah.’ I said to him, ‘Yet in your lands there is the doctrine of those who advocate the union of Creator and creation, which is worse than the doctrine of the philosophers?’ He replied, ‘No rational person would maintain what these people say. Indeed, every rational person recognizes the falsity of their doctrine.’ He meant that its falsity is evident, so it is not counted among matters that can confuse rational people, unlike the philosophers’ doctrine, which contains something rational, even though it is false.”

Tāj al-Dīn al-Anbārī, the distinguished Egyptian jurist, related to me that he heard Shaykh Ibrāhīm al-Ja‘barī say:

“I saw Ibn Arabī as an old man with a dyed beard. He is a filthy shaykh who disbelieves in every Book Allah has revealed and every prophet Allah has sent.”

Shaykh Rashīd al-Dīn Ibn al-Mu‘allim related to me:

“When I was a young man in Damascus, I used to hear people say of Ibn Arabī and al-Khusrawshāhī that both professed Islam while concealing unbelief”—or words to that effect.

He also related to me, on the authority of Shaykh Ibrāhīm al-Ja‘barī, that he was present with Ibn al-Fārid at his death, when he was reciting:

“If what I have met with marks my standing with you in love,
Then I have wasted my days.
A hope my soul attained for a time—
Today I deem it a tangle of dreams.”

The distinguished jurist Tāj al-Dīn al-Anbārī related to me that he heard Shaykh Ibrāhīm al-Ja‘barī say:

“I saw Ibn Arabī and Ibn al-Fārid in a dream as two blind old men, walking and stumbling, saying, ‘How do we find the way? Where is the way?’”

---

## Page 629

Shihāb al-Dīn al-Mizzī related to me from Sharaf al-Dīn, son of Shaykh Najm al-Dīn Ibn al-Hakīm, who reported that his father said:

“I arrived in Damascus just as Ibn Arabī died. I saw his funeral bier, and it looked as though ashes had been scattered over it. I saw that it did not resemble the funeral biers of the friends of Allah”—or he said—“Then I knew that this…”—or words to that effect.

He also related through his father that Shaykh Ismāʿīl al-Kūrānī used to say:

“Ibn Arabī is a devil.”

It was also related from him that he used to say of al-Harīrī, “He is a devil.”

Shihāb al-Dīn related to me from Judge Sharaf al-Dīn al-Bāzīlī that his father used to forbid him from engaging with the teachings of Ibn Arabī, Ibn al-Fārid, and Ibn Sabʿīn.

---

## Page 630

Section: Some Points That Reveal Their Disbelief and the Falsity of Their Doctrine

This is evident in several respects.

First: Their doctrine amounts to saying that Allah has neither created anything, nor originated it, nor brought it into being, nor fashioned it. For if no existence is there but His existence, it is impossible for Him to create His own existence or bring His own essence into being. That nothing creates itself is among the clearest and most immediately evident truths to reason. Hence He, glorified be He, says:

“Or were they created by nothing, or are they themselves the creators?”

They know that they were not created without a creator, and they know that nothing creates itself. It therefore necessarily follows that they have a creator. Yet according to these disbelieving, Pharaonic heretics, there is nothing that the Lord could have created, brought into being, or originated except His own holy Self. And His holy Self can only be created, subject to lordship, made, and brought into being; for this is impossible according to the self-evident principles of reason. This is among the most manifest forms of disbelief according to the adherents of all faiths and schools of thought.

As for the view of the author of al-Fusūs (The Bezels), there is nothing but His existence and the entities established in nonexistence, which are independent of Him. His existence cannot be created, and those entities are independent of Him; thus Allah has created nothing.

---

## Page 631

Second: According to them, Allah is neither the Lord of the worlds nor the Owner of Dominion, since nothing exists but His existence. He cannot be His own Lord, nor can a king who is owned be the king who owns. They have explicitly professed this disbelief, despite its contradiction, saying that He is the King of Dominion on the grounds that His existence depends upon the entities of things, while the entities of things depend upon His existence. Thus, things own His existence, and so He is the King of Dominion.

Third: According to them, Allah has never provided anyone with anything, given anyone anything, shown mercy to anyone, done good to anyone, or guided anyone. Nor has He bestowed any blessing upon anyone, taught anyone any knowledge, or taught anyone articulate speech. In short, according to them, nothing whatsoever has reached anyone from Him: neither good nor evil, benefit nor harm, giving nor withholding, guidance nor misguidance. All these things are His very Self and nothing but His existence. There is therefore no other being whom they could reach, no one besides Him who could benefit from them, and no servant who could be provided for, granted victory, or guided.

Furthermore, in the view of the author of al-Fusūs (The Bezels), these entities are established in nonexistence, and it is the entities themselves that have done good and evil, brought benefit and inflicted harm. This, according to him, is the secret of divine decree. In the view of the others, however, there is no established entity other than Him at all. Rather, He Himself reproaches Himself, He Himself curses Himself, and He Himself kills Himself. He is the one provided for, beaten, and insulted; He is both the active and passive partner in sexual intercourse, both the eater and the eaten. They have stated this plainly and explicitly.

Fourth: According to them, Allah is the one who bows, prostrates, humbles Himself, and worships

---

## Page 632

and fasts, goes hungry, stands, sleeps, suffers diseases and illnesses, is afflicted by enemies, undergoes trials, and endures severe hardship. They have stated this explicitly. They have also explicitly stated that whenever distress afflicts souls, it is He who suffers that distress, and that when He relieves distress, He is merely relieving Himself of it. For this reason, some of these people—who are among the most unbelieving of Allah’s creatures and the greatest in hypocrisy, heretical deviation, rebellion against Allah, and obstinate opposition to Him—disapproved of a person patiently enduring affliction, because, according to them, He is both the afflicted one and the one who inflicts affliction.

They have explicitly stated that He is characterized by every imperfection and defect, since there is no one besides Him who can be characterized by imperfections and defects. Thus, every defect, imperfection, disbelief, and sinfulness in the world characterizes Him, and no one else is characterized by it. All of them agree on this with regard to existence. The author of al-Fusūs (The Bezels) then says that this is established in nonexistence, whereas others say that nothing exists but the existence of the Real, who is characterized by these defects and failings.

(Fifth)

According to them, those who worshipped al-Lāt, al-‘Uzzā, and Manāt, the third and last; those who worshipped Wadd, Suwā‘, Yaghūth, Ya‘ūq, and Nasr; those who worshipped Sirius, the star, the sun, and the moon; those who worshipped the Messiah, Ezra, and the angels; and all others who worshipped idols and images—among the people of Noah, ‘Ād, Thamūd, Pharaoh’s people, the Children of Israel, and all the Arab polytheists—worshipped none but Allah. It is inconceivable, according to them, that they should worship anyone other than Allah. They have stated this explicitly in many places, such as the words of the author of al-Fusūs in the chapter on the Word of Noah:

---

## Page 633

“‘And they devised a mighty plot.’ For calling people to Allah is a stratagem directed at those being called: He was not absent at the beginning that people should be called to Him as their ultimate goal. ‘I call to Allah’—this is the very stratagem. ‘With insight’—which entails that the entire affair belongs to Him. Thus they answered him with a stratagem, just as he called them.”

Later, he says:

“In their plotting, they said: ‘Never abandon your gods; never abandon Wadd, Suwā‘, Yaghūth, Ya‘ūq, or Nasr.’ For if they abandoned them, their ignorance of the Real would be proportionate to the number of these they abandoned. The Real has a particular aspect in every object of worship, recognized by those who know it and unknown to those who do not. Concerning the followers of Muhammad: ‘Your Lord has decreed that you worship none but Him’—that is, He has determined it. The knowledgeable person knows whom he has worshipped and in what form He appeared to be worshipped. He knows that distinction and multiplicity are like the limbs in a physical form and the intangible faculties in a spiritual form. Thus, in every object of worship, he has worshipped none but Allah.

“The person of lower rank is one who imagines divinity in it. Were it not for this imagining, neither stone nor anything else would be worshipped. Hence the Most High said: ‘Say: Name them.’ Had they named them, they would have called them a stone, a tree, or a star. Had they been asked, ‘Whom have you worshipped?’ they would have replied, ‘One god.’ They would not say ‘Allah’ or ‘the God’ except according to what they imagined. Rather, he said: ‘This is a locus of divine manifestation that ought to be venerated’; thus he does not confine himself.

“The person of lower rank, who relies on imagination, says: ‘We worship them only so that they may bring us nearer to Allah.’ The person of higher rank, who possesses knowledge, says: ‘Your God is one God, so submit to Him’—wherever He manifests. ‘And give glad tidings to the humble’: those in whom the fire of their nature has died down, so they said ‘a god,’ not ‘nature.’”

He also says in the chapter on the Word of Aaron:

“Then Aaron said to Moses: ‘I feared that

---

## Page 634

you would say, “You have divided the Children of Israel”’—and thus make me responsible for their division. For worship of the calf had divided them: some worshipped it, following and imitating al-Sāmirī, while others refrained from worshipping it until Moses returned so that they could ask him about it. Aaron feared that their division would be attributed to him. Moses understood the matter better than Aaron, because he knew what the calf-worshippers had worshipped, knowing that Allah had decreed that none but Him should be worshipped. Whatever Allah decrees necessarily comes to pass. Moses therefore reproached his brother Aaron for rejecting what had occurred and for the narrowness of his understanding. For the knowledgeable person sees the Real in everything—indeed, he sees Him as the very essence of everything. Moses was thus educating Aaron in knowledge, although he was younger than him.

“Accordingly, when Aaron said what he did, Moses turned back to al-Sāmirī and said to him: ‘What, then, was your purpose, O Sāmirī?’ He meant what you had done in turning specifically to the form of the calf.”

He continued his discussion until he said:

“Thus Aaron’s inability to restrain them through action—to prevail over the calf-worshippers by exercising authority over the calf, as Moses exercised authority over it—was wisdom from Allah made manifest in existence, so that He might be worshipped in every form. Even if that form subsequently passed away, it did so only after assuming divinity in the eyes of its worshipper.

“For this reason, no kind of thing remained unworshipped, whether through deification or through subjection. This is inevitable, as anyone with understanding will recognize. Nothing in the world has been worshipped without first being invested with exalted status in the worshipper’s eyes and coming to occupy a rank in his heart.

---

## Page 635

And for this reason, the Real has named Himself for us “Exalted in ranks,” not “Exalted in rank.” He thus made the ranks manifold within a single essence, for He decreed that none but Him should be worshipped in many different ranks, each providing a divine locus of manifestation in which He was worshipped.

The greatest and highest locus of manifestation in which He is worshipped is desire, as He said:

“Have you seen the one who takes his own desire as his god?”

Desire is therefore the greatest object of worship, for nothing is worshipped except through it, whereas it is worshipped only through itself. Concerning this, I say:

By the truth of desire, desire is the cause of desire;
Were there no desire in the heart, desire would not be worshipped.

Do you not see how perfect Allah’s knowledge of all things is, and how He completed His statement concerning the one who worshipped his desire and took it as a god, saying:

“And Allah has led him astray with knowledge.”

Going astray is bewilderment. This arises from seeing that this worshipper worshipped nothing but his own desire, submitting obediently to whatever it commanded him concerning the worship of those particular beings he worshipped. Even the worship of Allah proceeded from desire: had he not felt desire for that Holy Presence—that is, a will accompanied by love—he would neither have worshipped Allah nor preferred Him to anything else.

Likewise, whoever worshipped any form in the world and took it as a god did so only through desire. The worshipper thus remains continually under the dominion of his desire. The objects of worship are then seen to vary from one worshipper to another: everyone who worships something declares those who worship anything else to be unbelievers. Whoever possesses even the slightest awareness is bewildered by the unity of desire—or rather, by its oneness, as mentioned—for it is one and the same essence in every worshipper.

“And Allah has led him astray”—that is, Allah has bewildered him with the knowledge that every worshipper worshipped nothing but his own desire, and nothing but his own desire enslaved him, whether

---

## Page 636

it accorded with what the religious law prescribed or not. The perfected knower is one who sees every object of worship as a locus of manifestation of the Real, who is worshipped therein. Thus, they all call it a god alongside its particular name: tree, stone, animal, human being, star, or angel. This name identifies its particular identity. Divinity is a rank that leads the worshipper to imagine that it belongs to his object of worship. In reality, it is a locus of manifestation of the Real to the sight of this worshipper, who remains devoted to this object of worship in this locus of manifestation particular to a stone.

For this reason, some who did not understand the import of their own words said in ignorance:

“We only worship them to bring us nearer to Allah.”

Yet they called them gods, as when they said:

“Has he made the gods one God? Indeed, this is an astonishing thing!”

They did not deny Him, but were astonished at this, for they remained attached to the multiplicity of forms and the attribution of divinity to them. The Messenger came and called them to a single God, known but not witnessed—as their own testimony showed that they acknowledged Him and believed in Him when they said:

“We only worship them to bring us nearer to Allah.”

For they knew that those forms were stones. Thus, the proof against them was established by His saying:

“Say, ‘Name them.’”

They could name them only by names they knew truly applied to them, such as stone, wood, star, and the like.

As for those who know the matter as it truly is, they outwardly disavow the forms that are worshipped. Their rank in knowledge requires them to submit to the rule of the time, because they are subject to the authority of the Messenger in whom they believe and through whom they are called believers. They are therefore worshippers of the time, despite knowing that they had not worshipped those forms themselves, but had worshipped Allah in them, under the sovereign power of self-manifestation

---

## Page 637

which those among them recognized, but which the denier, who has no knowledge of what manifests itself, did not know, and which the perfected knower—whether a prophet, a messenger, or an heir to them—concealed.

He therefore instructed them to withdraw from those forms when the Messenger of the time withdrew from them, following the Messenger and hoping that Allah would love them, in accordance with His words:

“Say, ‘If you love Allah, follow me, and Allah will love you.’”

Thus, he called them to a God to whom one turns, who is known in general terms but is not witnessed. Eyes cannot perceive Him, but He perceives the eyes, owing to His subtlety and His permeation of the individual realities of things. Eyes cannot perceive Him, just as they cannot perceive the spirits that govern their bodies and outward forms. He is the Subtle, the All-Aware. Experiential knowledge is tasting; tasting is self-manifestation; and self-manifestation occurs in forms. Hence, forms are indispensable, as is self-manifestation. Thus, whoever sees Him through his own desire must worship Him—if you have understood this.” End of quotation.

Reflect, then, on the true nature of what these people uphold:

They have all endorsed every form of polytheism in the world, set every created thing on an equal footing with Allah, and allowed anything whatsoever to be worshipped. Yet, while worshipping everything, they say: “We have worshipped none but Allah.”

Their teaching thus combines two things: every form of polytheism, and every form of denial and negation—all while they imagine that they have worshipped none but Allah. It is well known that this contradicts the religion of all the messengers, the religion of all the People of the Book and all religious communities, and even the religion of the polytheists. It also contradicts the innate disposition upon which Allah created His servants: what they understand in their hearts and find within themselves. It represents the utmost corruption, contradiction, sophistry, and denial of the Lord of all worlds.

For it is self-evidently known that the messengers regarded what the polytheists worshipped as

---

## Page 638

other than Allah, and regarded their worshippers as worshipping something other than Allah, associating partners with Him, setting others on an equal footing with Him, and assigning Him a rival. For they called humanity to worship Allah alone, without any partner. This is Allah’s religion, with which He revealed His Books and sent His messengers. It is Islam in its universal sense: Allah accepts no other religion from earlier or later generations, and He does not forgive anyone who abandons it after the message has reached him. As He said:

“Indeed, Allah does not forgive associating partners with Him, but He forgives anything less than that for whom He wills.”

This is what distinguishes the people of Paradise from the people of the Fire, and the blessed from the wretched. As the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“Whoever’s last words are ‘There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah’ is assured of Paradise.”

He also said:

“Whoever dies knowing that there is no deity worthy of worship except Allah is assured of Paradise.”

And he said:

“I certainly know a declaration which no servant utters at the time of death without his soul finding comfort in it; it is the foundation of the religion.”

Likewise, he said:

“I have been commanded to fight the people until they testify that there is no deity worthy of worship except Allah and that I am Allah’s Messenger. When they say this, their lives and property are protected from me, except as required by its rightful demands, and their reckoning rests with Allah.”

The virtues of this declaration, the truths it embodies, and its place in the religion surpass what anyone can describe or comprehend. It is the essence of the entire matter. As Allah Most High said:

“And We sent no messenger before you without revealing to him: ‘There is no deity worthy of worship except Me, so worship Me.’”

He, glorified be He, thus states that He reveals to every messenger the denial of divinity to anything besides Him and its affirmation for Him alone. Yet these heretical polytheists claim that everything is as entitled to divinity as Allah is. Allah Most High said:

“Ask those of Our messengers whom We sent before you: Have We appointed, besides

---

## Page 639

the Most Merciful, deities to be worshipped?” Yet these heretics claim that everything is a deity that is worshipped. He, glorified be He, declares that He has appointed no deities besides the Most Merciful. Allah Most High also said:

“We certainly sent a messenger to every nation, saying: ‘Worship Allah and shun false objects of worship.’”

Allah, glorified be He, thus commanded that He be worshipped and that false objects of worship be shunned. According to these people, however, Allah is within all such objects, or they are Allah, and whoever worships them worships none but Allah. Allah Most High said:

“O mankind, worship your Lord, who created you and those before you”—the two verses.

He, glorified be He, commanded people to worship the Lord who created these signs. According to these accursed heretics, however, He is identical with these signs. He, glorified be He, forbade people to set up rivals to Him. Yet according to them, this is inconceivable, for those rivals are identical with Him: how, then, could He be His own rival? Those who worshipped these rivals therefore worshipped none but Him.

Furthermore, these heretics argued from the polytheists’ calling what they worshipped gods, as when they said:

“Has he made the gods into one God?”

They believed that, because the polytheists called them gods, this designation was evidence that they truly possessed divinity.

Allah has refuted this argument of the polytheists in several places. For example, He, glorified be He, relates that Hūd said to the polytheists among his people:

“Do you argue with me about names that you and your forefathers have given?”—the verse.

This refutes their statement:

“Have you come to us so that we may worship Allah alone and abandon what our forefathers worshipped?”

Thus Allah’s Messenger, peace and blessings be upon him, explained that their calling them gods

---

## Page 640

and objects of worship was a designation invented by them and their forefathers, for which Allah had sent down neither proof nor authority. Judgment belongs to Allah alone. He, glorified be He, has commanded that none but Him be worshipped. How, then, can the words of polytheists who have no proof be cited as evidence, when Allah has refuted their claim? He has commanded His creatures to worship Him alone, rather than these idols that the polytheists called gods. Yet according to the heretics, those who worship idols have worshipped none but Allah.

Furthermore, the polytheists objected to the Messenger when he came to them so that they would worship Allah alone and abandon what their forefathers had worshipped. If they had always been worshipping Allah alone, as the heretics claim, then they would not have been called to abandon what their forefathers worshipped. Rather, he and the other prophets would have come to them so that everything their forefathers worshipped would be worshipped.

Likewise, He, glorified be He, relates Joseph’s words in the surah of Joseph:

“O my two fellow prisoners! Are many separate lords better, or Allah, the One, the All-Prevailing? You worship nothing besides Him but names that you and your forefathers have given, for which Allah has sent down no authority.”

Up to His words:

“But most people do not know.”

He, glorified be He, also said:

“Have you considered al-Lāt and al-ʿUzzā, and Manāt, the third and last?”

Up to His words:

“Yet guidance has certainly come to them from their Lord.”

These three mentioned in this surah were the great, prominent idols that the polytheists would regularly visit from their towns. Al-Lāt was on the coast, opposite Qudayd

---

## Page 641

for the people of Medina; al-‘Uzzā was near ‘Arafāt for the people of Mecca, and Manāt was in Tā’if for Thaqīf. These three towns are the principal urban centres of the Hijāz.

He, glorified be He, declared that the names the polytheists gave them were names they had invented, with no reality behind them. They worship only names with no corresponding realities, for the things so named possess nothing of divinity, might, or the power to ordain. Allah has sent down no authority for these names. The polytheists follow nothing but conjecture—which is of no avail against the truth—in believing that these are gods capable of bringing benefit or harm, and they follow their own desires. Yet according to the heretics, when they worship their desires, they have worshipped Allah.

He, glorified be He, relates that the foremost of all leaders, the close friend of the Most Merciful, and the best of creation after Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said to his father:

“O my father! Why do you worship what neither hears nor sees, nor can benefit you in any way?”

“O my father! There has come to me knowledge which has not come to you.”

Up to His words:

“And so become an ally of Satan.”

Thus, he forbade his father to worship idols that neither hear nor see nor benefit him in any way, and rebuked him for doing so. But according to these heretics’ claim—that in every object of worship they worship none but Allah—it would follow that Allah is the One who neither hears nor sees nor benefits him in any way. He would also be the One whom he forbade his father to worship and the One whom he commanded him to worship.

Likewise, the most accomplished of their false idols, the wicked al-Tilimsānī, said in a poem:
