Indeed, the leading hadith scholars, such as al-Dāraqutnī, Abū Nuʿaym, and Abū Muhammad al-Khallāl, devoted separate works to its merit, and the compilers of the authentic hadith collections included numerous hadiths concerning it. In this surah, He says:

“Say, ‘He is Allah, the One;
Allah, the Eternal Refuge.
He neither begets nor is born,
nor is there to Him any equivalent.’”

This surah is the foundation upon which the imams rely in affirming Allah’s oneness, including Imam Ahmad, al-Fudayl ibn ʿIyād, and other imams before and after them. He thus denies that He has origins, offspring, or counterparts. These encompass all the relationships in which created beings stand—humans, animals, angels, jinn, and even plants and the like. For

---

## Page 821

every created thing necessarily has something related to it: an origin, offspring, or a counterpart—or two or all three of these. This is evident in humans, jinn, and animals. As for the angels, although they do not reproduce through procreation, they have counterparts and others like them. Hence He, Glorified be He, says:

“And of all things We created pairs, so that you may take heed.”

“So flee to Allah.”

Some of the early generations said: “So that you may take heed and realize that the Creator of pairs is One.” Thus, this surah refutes those among the Jews, Christians, Sabians, Magians, and polytheists who disbelieved. His words:

“He does not beget”

refute the claim of those who say that He has sons and daughters among the angels or human beings, such as those who say that the angels are Allah’s daughters, or that the Messiah or Ezra is Allah’s son. As He, Most High, says concerning them:

“They have made the jinn partners with Allah, although He created them, and have falsely attributed sons and daughters to Him without knowledge.”

He, Most High, also says:

“So ask them: Does your Lord have daughters while they have sons?
Or did We create the angels female while they were witnesses?
Indeed, it is out of their falsehood that they say,
‘Allah has begotten,’ and they are certainly liars.
Has He chosen daughters over sons?
What is the matter with you? How do you judge?
Will you not then take heed?
Or do you have clear authority?
Then bring your scripture, if you are truthful.
They have claimed a kinship between Him and the jinn, yet the jinn know that they will surely be brought forth.”

He, Most High, also says:

“The Jews say, ‘Ezra is the son of Allah,’ and the Christians say, ‘The Messiah is the son of Allah.’ That is what they say with their mouths, imitating the words of those who disbelieved before them. May Allah destroy them! How deluded they are!”

“They have taken

---

## Page 822

their rabbis and monks as lords besides Allah, and the Messiah, son of Mary.” He has stated that this imitates the words of those who disbelieved before them.

It has been said that these were their own predecessors. It has also been said that they were the Arab polytheists. Both interpretations are open to question: the Arab polytheists who said this did not precede the Jews and Christians, while their own predecessors belonged to those same communities. Perhaps, then, the reference is to the polytheistic Sabians who lived before Moses and the Messiah in Syria, Egypt, and elsewhere, and who regarded the angels as His offspring, as we shall explain.

He, Most High, says:

“They attribute to Allah what they themselves dislike, and their tongues falsely claim that the best is theirs.”

This refers to the claim of those Arabs who said that the angels were Allah’s daughters.

He, Most High, says:

“They assign to what they do not know a share of what We have provided for them. By Allah, you will surely be questioned about what you used to fabricate.”

“They attribute daughters to Allah—glory be to Him—while they have what they desire.”

“When one of them is given news of a daughter, his face darkens, and he is choked with grief.”

“He hides from his people because of the bad news he has received. Should he keep her in humiliation or bury her in the dust? Evil indeed is their judgment!”

“To those who do not believe in the Hereafter belongs the description of evil, while to Allah belongs the highest description. He is the Almighty, the All-Wise.”

He, Most High, says:

“Yet they have made some of His servants a part of Him. Indeed, man is clearly ungrateful.”

“Or has He taken daughters from among those He creates and favored you with sons?”

“When one of them is given news of what he attributes to the Most Merciful, his face darkens, and he is choked with grief.”

“Or one brought up amid ornaments, unable to argue clearly in a dispute?”

“They have made the angels, who are servants of the Most Merciful, females. Did they witness their creation? Their testimony will be recorded, and they will be questioned.”

---

## Page 823

This same fault for which Allah reproached those Arabs who regarded the angels as His daughters, despite their aversion to having daughters themselves, has its counterpart among the Christians. They attribute a child to Allah, yet consider the leading figures of their religion too exalted to have a wife or child. Thus, they attribute to Allah what they deem unworthy of their own religious leaders.

He, Most High, says:

“They say, ‘The Most Merciful has taken a child.’”

“You have indeed made a monstrous claim.”

“The heavens almost rupture at it, the earth splits apart, and the mountains collapse in devastation,”

“because they attribute a child to the Most Merciful.”

“It is not fitting for the Most Merciful to take a child.”

“There is no one in the heavens and the earth who will not come to the Most Merciful as a servant.”

“He has counted them and numbered them precisely.”

“And each of them will come to Him alone on the Day of Resurrection.”

He, Most High, says:

“O People of the Scripture, do not go to extremes in your religion, and say nothing about Allah except the truth. The Messiah, Jesus, son of Mary, was only a messenger of Allah, His word which He conveyed to Mary, and a spirit from Him. So believe in Allah and His messengers, and do not say, ‘Three.’ Desist—it is better for you. Allah is only one God. Glory be to Him! He is far above having a child. To Him belongs whatever is in the heavens and whatever is on the earth. And sufficient is Allah as a Guardian.”

“The Messiah would never disdain to be a servant of Allah, nor would the angels brought near to Him. Whoever disdains His worship and is arrogant—He will gather them all to Himself.”

“As for those who believe and do righteous deeds, He will give them their rewards in full and grant them more out of His bounty. But as for those who disdain and are arrogant, He will punish them with a painful punishment, and they will find no protector or helper besides Allah.”

Thus, He forbade the People of the Scripture to go to extremes in religion or to say anything about Allah except the truth,

---

## Page 824

and stated the truth concerning the Messiah, then said to them:

“So believe in Allah and His messengers.”

For they disbelieved in Allah through their doctrine of the Trinity, and disbelieved in His messengers through their doctrines of union and indwelling. Thus, they rejected the two foundations of Islam in its universal sense: testifying that Allah alone is worthy of worship, and testifying that the messengers are His messengers. He also stated that the Messiah and the angels do not disdain to worship Him, because some people regarded the angels as His children, just as they did the Messiah, and worshipped both the angels and the Messiah.

For this reason, He said:

“It is not for a human being whom Allah has given the Scripture, wisdom and prophethood to then say to people, ‘Be servants of mine rather than Allah.’ Rather, ‘Be godly scholars through your teaching of the Scripture and your study of it.’”

“Nor would he command you to take the angels and the prophets as lords. Would he command you to disbelieve after you have submitted to Allah?”

Thus, He mentioned both the angels and the prophets. In His Book, He has denied both that He begets and that He takes a child.

He said:

“And say, ‘Praise be to Allah, who has not taken a child, who has no partner in sovereignty, and who needs no protector because of weakness.’”

He, Most High, also said:

“Allah has not taken any child, nor is there any god alongside Him”—and so on to the end of the verse.

He said:

“He to whom belongs the dominion of the heavens and the earth, who has not taken a child and has no partner in sovereignty.”

He said:

“We did not create the heaven and the earth and all that is between them in play.”

“Had We wished to take a diversion, We would have taken it from what is with Us—if We were to do so.”

“Rather, We hurl the truth against falsehood, and it crushes it; then falsehood vanishes. And woe to you for what you attribute to Us!”

“To Him belongs whoever is in the heavens and the earth. Those who are with Him are not too proud to worship Him, nor do they grow weary.”

“They glorify Him night and day, without slackening.”

“Or have they taken gods

---

## Page 825

from the earth who can raise the dead?”

“Had there been gods other than Allah in the heavens and the earth, both would have fallen into ruin. Glory be to Allah, Lord of the Throne, far above what they attribute to Him!”

He also said:

“They say, ‘The Most Merciful has taken a child.’ Glory be to Him! Rather, they are honoured servants.”

“They do not speak before He speaks, and they act by His command.”

“He knows what is before them and what is behind them. They intercede only for those with whom He is pleased, and they stand in awe of Him.”

It is well known that those who fabricated sons and daughters for Him without knowledge, those who said, “Allah has begotten”—and they are surely liars—and those who said, “The Messiah is the son of Allah” and “Ezra is the son of Allah”: the rational among them did not mean physical birth of the kind found among animals, whereby a portion separates from the male and enters the female, from which offspring is formed.

The Christians and the Sabians agree in denying this. Likewise, I do not think that the rational among the Arab polytheists believed this. Rather, they described an intellectual, spiritual begetting, as when the Christians say that the substance which is God in one respect and the Word in another clothed itself in a human being created from Mary. Thus, they say that divinity clothed itself in humanity. Its outward aspect—the armour and the shirt—is human, while its inward aspect—the one wearing that armour—is divine: it is the Son, who is the Word, because it is begotten of the Father, who is the substance of being.

In their view, this sonship rests upon two principles:

First: the substance which is the Word is begotten of the substance which is the Father, just as knowledge and speech arise from one who knows and speaks.

---

## Page 826

Second: this substance united with the Messiah and clothed itself in him as in armour. That substance is the Father in one respect and the Son in another.

For this reason, Allah reports them as saying, at one point, “The Messiah is the son of Allah,” and at another, “Allah is the Messiah, son of Mary.” As for His report that they said, “Allah is the third of three,” the Quran commentators say that this means Allah, the Messiah and his mother, as He said:

“O Jesus, son of Mary, did you tell people, ‘Take me and my mother as two gods besides Allah’?”

For this reason, He said in the same passage:

“The Messiah, son of Mary, was no more than a messenger; other messengers had passed away before him. His mother was a woman of truth.”

That is, the Messiah’s highest rank is that of a messenger, and his mother’s highest rank is that of a woman of truth; neither attains divinity. This is the evidence for that interpretation, and it is clear. Some people claim that what is meant is the three hypostases—the Father, the Son and the Holy Spirit—but this interpretation is open to question.

As for His words:

“They attribute the jinn to Allah as partners, though He created them, and without knowledge they fabricate sons and daughters for Him. Glory be to Him! Exalted is He above what they attribute to Him!”

“Originator of the heavens and the earth! How could He have a child when He has no consort? He created all things, and He has knowledge of all things.”

His words, “Originator of the heavens and the earth,” mean that He originated them both, as He similarly stated in al-Baqarah. They do not mean that His heavens and earth are wondrous, although Arabic would allow that reading were it not for the context. For the purpose is to refute their fabricated claims of sons and daughters for Him, and their claim that He has taken a child.

---

## Page 827

This claim is negated by the contrary fact that He originated the heavens. Then He said:

“How could He have a child?”

He gave three proofs against this. The first is that He has no consort; this rules out birth in its familiar sense. His words:

“He created all things”

rule out birth in the intellectual sense, namely generation, because His creation of all things is incompatible with their being generated from Him.

As for His words:

“And He has knowledge of all things,”

it appears—and Allah knows best—that, since the Christians claimed that what united with the Messiah was the Word, which they interpret as knowledge, while the Sabians who uphold generation and causation do not regard Him as knowing all things, He stated that He has knowledge of all things to affirm this attribute for Himself in refutation of the Sabians, and to deny it to anyone else in refutation of the Christians.

If this is so, the doctrine of those who hold that the intellects and souls—which they claim are the angels—are generated from Him is more explicit than the Christians’ doctrine in asserting that He begot the angels and that they are His sons and daughters: the intellects are His sons, and the souls His daughters.

This also includes those professing Islam who have adopted philosophy. Indeed, I know one of their leading figures who, when asked about the intellect and the soul, said, “They are like male and female.” He thus likened them to a son and a daughter. They hold that these are generated from Him as an effect is generated from its cause: He cannot separate His essence from His effect, nor His effect from Himself, any more than He can separate Himself from Himself. It is like the relation of the sun’s rays to the sun, only more inseparable still.

---

## Page 828

These people also say that the spirits He has begotten are connected to the celestial spheres—the sun, the moon and the stars—just as divinity is connected to the body of the Messiah. They therefore worship them as the Christians worshipped the Messiah, though their unbelief is greater in many respects. They are more rightly described as polytheists than the Christians, for they worship what they know to be separate from Allah, neither Allah Himself nor one of His attributes. The Christians, by contrast, claim that they worship only what has united with Allah, not the effects He has begotten.

Those who worshipped the angels, the stars, and human spirits and bodies then fashioned idols according to their forms and natures. This was among the greatest causes of idol worship.

For this reason, Abraham, Allah’s intimate friend and the leader of those devoted to pure monotheism, addressed those who worshipped the stars, the sun and the moon, and those who worshipped idols, while associating partners with Allah and acknowledging the source of them all. Allah has related their story in several places in the Quran. These were the polytheistic Sabians ruled by Nimrod.

Their scholars were the philosophers among the Greeks and others who lived in Syria, Upper Mesopotamia, Iraq and elsewhere, as well as on the islands of the sea, before the Christians. They inhabited these lands in the days of the Children of Israel, and it was they who fought the Children of Israel, sometimes prevailing and sometimes being defeated. Sennacherib, Nebuchadnezzar and others like them were kings of the Sabians after Abraham, whereas Nimrod lived in his time.

---

## Page 829

This makes clear how the Quran refutes the doctrines of those who lived before this community, and of the unbelievers and hypocrites within it, who attribute begetting to Allah. Yet many people do not understand how the Quran addresses these doctrines, for this requires two things: understanding what their doctrine means, rather than merely its wording, and understanding the meaning of the Quran, then bringing the two together. You will then find that the Quran addresses and invalidates the very meaning they intended.

As for the union of the offspring, it is interpreted as begetting itself. It falls under acts, not attributes, as a group of Christians say concerning the Messiah.

---

## Page 830

Section

This, then, denies that He—glory be to Him—is the father of anything or has taken anything as a child, in any conceivable form of begetting or taking a child.

As for denying that He was begotten, this includes denying that He originated through any kind of generation from any human being, or belongs among any other beings that originate from another. It thus refutes those who say that the Messiah is Allah. It also refutes the Dajjāl, who claims that he is Allah, and those who say that a human being is Allah: the extremists of this community who make this claim concerning ʿAlī, certain members of the Prophet’s household, or certain shaykhs. Some have said this of ʿAlī and a number of members of the Prophet’s household; they have also said it of the prophets. Others have said it of al-Hallāj, others of al-Hākim in Egypt, others of Shaykh ʿAdī, and others of Yūnus al-Qanīnī. Some extend this claim to shaykhs generally and endorse all these claims.

Thus His statement—glory be to Him—

“Nor was He begotten”

negates all of this, for all these people were begotten, whereas Allah was not begotten.

For this reason, when Allah mentions the Messiah in the Quran, He calls him “son of Mary,” unlike the other prophets, as in His statement:

“They have certainly disbelieved who say, ‘Allah is the Messiah.’”

And His statement:

“The Messiah, son of Mary, was no more than a messenger; messengers had passed away before him.”

And His statement:

---

## Page 831

“When Allah said, ‘O Jesus, son of Mary, remember My favour upon you and upon your mother.’”

And His statement:

“O Jesus, son of Mary, did you tell people, ‘Take me and my mother as two gods besides Allah’?”

And His statement:

“And We made the son of Mary and his mother a sign.”

And His statement:

“And their saying, ‘We have killed the Messiah, Jesus, son of Mary, the messenger of Allah.’”

There are two benefits in this. The first is to make clear that he was begotten, whereas Allah was not begotten. The second is to identify him as Mary’s son, not the son of Allah.

As for His statement:

“The Messiah would never disdain”—and the rest of the verse—and His statement:

“The Jews say, ‘Ezra is the son of Allah,’ and the Christians say, ‘The Messiah is the son of Allah,’”

He was reporting what they themselves said. They attributed him to Allah as His son, so they did not include in their statement the words “the Messiah, son of Mary.”

His statement:

“Nor is there anyone equal to Him”

negates partners and rivals. This encompasses anyone who makes something equal to Allah in any matter exclusive to lordship, such as bringing creation into existence, or to divinity, such as being worshipped, being invoked, and the like. These points demonstrate that the Book of Allah refutes the claims of anyone who believes that a human being is divine, whether through union, indwelling, or otherwise.

---

## Page 832

Section

As for these heretics, their unbelief is not confined to claiming that He begot something, took a son, or is a human being who was born, by virtue of the Lord’s union with him. All of this entails affirming two distinct entities, one of which united with the other or dwelt within it. Such a view is held only by those who profess a particular, restricted union or a particular, restricted indwelling.

These people, however, maintain that there is nothing other than Him or besides Him; that He has created nothing; that He is neither the Lord nor the Owner of anything; and that He has no servant or worshipper, no supplicant who invokes Him and whom He answers, no distressed person who turns to Him in dire need and whom He answers, and no petitioner who asks of Him and whom He answers. A servant perceives these distinctions only when he is veiled from witnessing absolute unity in his imagination.

According to them, once the veil over his heart is lifted, he sees that there are no two distinct entities in any respect whatsoever, such that one could be the Creator and the other a creature, one a worshipper and the other the Lord, one a parent and the other an offspring, or one a partner to the other or an intercessor with Him, through whose worship one might draw nearer to Him.

---

## Page 833

This is the position of the most adept among them, such as al-Tilimsānī and Ibn al-Fārid; al-Tilimsānī has a deeper understanding of what their position truly entails. As for Ibn Arabī, he says:

“All of this concerns essences fixed in nonexistence, not anything that exists. As for existence itself, it is inconceivable that within it there should be a Lord and a servant, a Creator and a creature, or one who supplicates and one who answers. Rather, when existence flowed over the entities and manifested in them, distinctions arose on account of those entities, just as light becomes differentiated in glass because of the glass’s differing colors.”

The Quran refutes these people in countless passages. The accounts Allah relates concerning Pharaoh, who is their leader, also contain a refutation of them. Pharaoh denied the Lord of the worlds and denied that Moses had a God whom one could look upon; he did not deny this existence, which is the world.

Likewise, these people acknowledge only this existence, which is this world. In their view, there is nothing besides it, and they say: “It is Allah, and it is the great man.”

---

## Page 834

And Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah sanctify his soul—said:

In the name of Allah, the Most Merciful, the Especially Merciful.

From Ahmad ibn Taymiyya:

To the shaykh Abū al-Fath Nasr, a man of spiritual knowledge, an exemplar, a follower of the spiritual path, and a devout worshipper. May Allah grant him, inwardly and outwardly, the blessings He has granted the hearts of His close servants, and aid him against the devils among humankind and jinn in his public and private life. May He make clear through him the path of Muhammad, which accords with his revealed law, and disclose through him the reality of religion that distinguishes between His creation and obedience to Him, and between His will and His love. Thus may people clearly see the distinction between words pertaining to creation and words pertaining to religion, and between sincere, righteous believers and the hypocrites who imitate them, just as Allah has distinguished between them in His Book and His Sunnah.

To proceed: Allah Most High has bestowed upon the shaykh—and, through him, upon others—inward and outward blessings in religious and worldly affairs. He has granted him a lofty standing and divinely bestowed affection among those distinguished Muslims who seek neither exaltation on earth nor corruption, because of what he has been granted

---

## Page 835

by Allah Most High in the form of sound understanding and upright intention. For knowledge and will are the foundation of the path of guidance and worship. Allah sent Muhammad—may Allah Most High bless him and grant him peace—with the most perfect love grounded in the most perfect knowledge. Through love for Allah and His Messenger—which is the foundation of deeds—love involving associating partners with Allah and a lack of clarity was excluded. As Allah Most High says:

“Among people are those who take others besides Allah as equals, loving them as they love Allah. But those who believe are stronger in their love for Allah.”

And He Most High says:

“Say, ‘If your fathers, your sons, your brothers, your spouses, your clan, wealth you have acquired, commerce whose decline you fear, and homes you cherish are dearer to you than Allah and His Messenger and striving in His cause, then wait until Allah brings about His command.’”

Thus, love grounded in faith gives rise to the savor of faith and spiritual joy grounded in religion. As reported in the two Sahīh collections, Anas said that the Messenger of Allah—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“Whoever possesses three qualities will find the sweetness of faith in his heart: that Allah and His Messenger are dearer to him than anything else; that he loves a person only for Allah’s sake; and that he hates returning to unbelief after Allah has saved him from it as much as he hates being cast into the Fire.”

He—may Allah Most High bless him and grant him peace—thus made experiencing the sweetness of faith dependent upon a surpassing love for Allah and His Messenger, upon love for His sake—for Allah’s sake—and upon hatred of the opposite of faith.

In Sahīh Muslim, al-‘Abbās reported that the Messenger of Allah—may Allah Most High bless him and grant him peace—said:

“He has tasted the flavor of faith who is pleased with Allah as his Lord, Islam as his religion, and Muhammad as his Messenger.”

---

## Page 836

He thus made tasting the flavor of faith dependent upon being pleased with these foundational principles, just as he made spiritual joy dependent upon love. This was so that he—may Allah Most High bless him and grant him peace—might distinguish, in matters of spiritual taste and joy—which underlie outward deeds and are the fruit of inward deeds—between what Allah and His Messenger commanded and other things. As Sahl ibn ‘Abd Allāh al-Tustarī said:

“Any spiritual joy unsupported by the Quran and Sunnah is false.”

For everyone who loves something experiences a taste corresponding to that love. Hence Allah Most High challenged those claiming to love Him with His words:

“If you love Allah, then follow me; Allah will love you and forgive you your sins.”

Al-Hasan al-Basrī said: “Some people in the time of the Messenger of Allah—may Allah Most High bless him and grant him peace—claimed that they loved Allah, so He challenged them with this verse.” Thus, He made the servant’s love for Allah entail following His Messenger, and made following His Messenger entail the Lord’s love for His servant.

He described those who love Him in His words:

“Allah will bring forth a people whom He loves and who love Him, humble toward the believers, mighty against the disbelievers, striving in the cause of Allah and fearing no blame from any critic.”

He thus described those who love Him and are loved by Him with the same perfection with which He described His Messenger: combining the qualities of majesty and beauty that were separated in the two religions before ours. These are firmness and might against Allah’s enemies, and humility and mercy toward the allies of Allah and His Messenger.

Yet many people experience spiritual joy and a love that is merely general and unrestricted, as one of their leading figures described such a person:

“Driven from his homeland … banished from his dwelling

---

## Page 837

Weeping over ruins and abandoned camps … loving, yet knowing not whom.”

As for the shaykh—may Allah be good to him—Allah has endowed him with light and knowledge—the foundation of love and will—that distinguish the clearly defined love rooted in faith and the way of Muhammad from generalized love shared by others. Just as this lack of distinction occurs in love, it also occurs in affirming Allah’s oneness. Allah Most High says in the Mother of the Book, which the servant is required to recite in every prayer:

“You alone we worship, and You alone we ask for help.”

It is established in an authentic hadith that Allah says:

“I have divided the prayer between Myself and My servant into two halves: half is Mine and half is My servant’s, and My servant shall have what he asks for. When the servant says, ‘All praise is for Allah, Lord of all worlds,’ Allah says, ‘My servant has praised Me.’ When he says, ‘The Most Compassionate, the Most Merciful,’ Allah says, ‘My servant has extolled Me.’ When he says, ‘Master of the Day of Judgment,’ He says, ‘My servant has glorified Me,’ or He says, ‘My servant has entrusted his affairs to Me.’ When he says, ‘You alone we worship, and You alone we ask for help,’ He says, ‘This verse is divided into two halves between Myself and My servant, and My servant shall have what he asks for.’ When he says, ‘Guide us to the straight path,’ ‘The path of those You have blessed, not those who have incurred wrath, nor those who have gone astray,’ He says, ‘These are for My servant, and My servant shall have what he asks for.’”

For this reason, it has been reported that Allah revealed one hundred and four books, gathering their meanings in the Quran, the meanings of the Quran in the Mufassal section, the meanings of the Mufassal section in the Mother of the Book, and the meanings of

---

## Page 838

the Mother of the Book in these two statements:

“You alone we worship, and You alone we ask for help.”

Allah has reiterated this meaning in such statements as:

“So worship Him and put your trust in Him.”

And:

“In Him I put my trust, and to Him I turn.”

And His statement:

“In Him I put my trust, and to Him I turn in repentance.”

The Prophet—may Allah Most High bless him and grant him peace—used to say during his rites of worship:

“O Allah, this is from You and for You.”

He—glory be to Him—is therefore worthy of being singled out in devotion, which consists of invoking Him and devoting religion sincerely to Me: invocation through worship, expressed in love, turning to Him, obedience, reverence, honor, fear, hope, and other expressions of devotion to Him and worship of Him; and invocation through petition and seeking help, expressed in trusting Him, taking refuge in Him, asking of Him, and other such requests concerning what He—glory be to Him—does by virtue of His lordship. He—glory be to Him—is the First and the Last, the Inward and the Outward.

For this reason, the perfect revealed Law prescribes the name “Allah” in worship and the name “Lord” in petition. Thus, one who prays or remembers Allah says: “Allah is greatest,” “Glory be to Allah,” “All praise is for Allah,” and “There is no god but Allah.” Likewise, the words of the call to prayer begin, “Allah is greatest, Allah is greatest,” and continue to its end, along with other similar expressions.

In petition, one says:

“Our Lord, we have wronged ourselves.”

“My Lord, forgive me and my parents.”

“My Lord, because of the favor You have bestowed upon me, I will never support the criminals.”

“My Lord, I have wronged myself, so forgive me.”

“Our Lord, forgive us our sins and our excesses in our conduct, and make our steps firm.”

“My Lord, forgive and have mercy, for You are the best of those who show mercy.”

And other similar supplications.

---

## Page 839

Many of those who turn to Allah and tread the spiritual path perceive, in the course of their journey, His lordship and His perfect, all-encompassing sustaining power over every created thing, both entities and attributes. All these things subsist through Allah’s words by which creation is brought into being—the words in which the Prophet—may Allah Most High bless him and grant him peace—used to seek refuge, saying:

“I seek refuge in Allah’s perfect words, beyond which neither the righteous nor the wicked can pass, from the evil of what He has created, brought forth, and fashioned; from the evil of what descends from heaven and what ascends into it; from the evil of what He has brought forth in the earth and what emerges from it; from the evil of the trials of night and day; and from the evil of every night visitor except one who comes with good, O Most Merciful.”

Through this recognition of Allah’s oneness in lordship, such a person becomes wholly absorbed and loses awareness of what he is also commanded and required to do: affirming Allah’s exclusive right to worship, which He, the True One, loves and is pleased with. This means worshipping Him alone, without any partner; obeying Him and His Messenger; enjoining what He has commanded and forbidding what He has prohibited; and loving and hating for His sake. Whoever turns away from this affirmation and embraces only the former resembles the polytheistic Qadariyyah, who said:

“If Allah had willed, neither we nor our fathers would have associated partners with Him.”

Whoever embraces the latter without the former belongs to the Magian Qadariyyah, who claim that Allah did not create His servants’ actions and did not will everything that comes into being. This is what the Mu'tazilah and the Rāfidah maintain, and it also occurs in (the discourse of many speculative theologians and jurists. The former position has been adopted by antinomian groups who have cast off the commands and prohibitions. They invoke it only when it suits their desires; otherwise, it cannot be consistently maintained. It is widespread among devotional practitioners

---

## Page 840

who have departed from the Sharia, khafw al-'aduww (1) and others; for their ascetic practices and acts of worship include things that have not been commanded. These produce spiritual states in them, some of which are corrupt. In certain respects, they resemble monks and idol worshippers.

For this reason, Shaykh 'Abd al-Qādir—may Allah sanctify his soul—said:

“Many men, when they enter into the matter of divine decree and predestination, hold back. But a window into it was opened for me, and I resisted the decrees of the True One through the True One, for the sake of the True One. The friend of Allah is one who resists the decree, not one who simply conforms to it.”

In saying this, the Shaykh spoke in accordance with the way of Muhammad: the Muslim is commanded to do what Allah has commanded and to ward off what Allah has forbidden, even though its causes have been decreed. Thus, he repels Allah’s decree by Allah’s decree, as stated in the hadith that al-Tabarānī narrated in his Book of Supplication from the Prophet—may Allah Most High bless him and grant him peace:

“Indeed, supplication and affliction meet between heaven and earth.”

And in al-Tirmidhī:

“He was asked, ‘O Messenger of Allah, what do you think of the medicines we use for treatment, the healing recitations we seek, and the precautions we take for protection? Do they repel anything of Allah’s decree?’ He replied, ‘They are themselves part of Allah’s decree.’”

These two meanings are also indicated by the hadith that al-Tabarānī narrated from the Prophet—may Allah Most High bless him and grant him peace—in which he said:

“Allah says: ‘O son of Adam, there are just four matters: one is for Me, one is for you, one is between Me and you, and one is between you and My creation. As for

> **Footnotes:**
> (1) Thus in the original.

---

## Page 841

the matter that is for Me: you are to worship Me without associating anything with Me. As for the matter that is for you, it is your deeds: I shall reward you for them when you are most in need of that reward. As for the matter between Me and you, yours is to supplicate and Mine is to answer. As for the matter between you and My creation, treat people as you would like them to treat you.’”

With regard to affirming Allah’s oneness—whether this encompasses both His sole right to worship and His lordship, or either of them—the servant has three stations.

The first is the station of distinction and multiplicity, through His bounty in the multitude of created beings and commanded acts.

The second is the station of unified awareness and self-effacement: he becomes so absorbed in the One he witnesses that he loses awareness of his witnessing; in the One he worships, awareness of his worship; in the One whose oneness he affirms, awareness of his affirmation; in the One he remembers, awareness of his remembrance; and in the One he loves, awareness of his love. This is the effacement of awareness of anything other than Allah, and it is the effacement of those who fall short.

Complete self-effacement, however, in accordance with Muhammad’s way, consists in abandoning worship of anything other than Allah, seeking aid from anything other than Him, and seeking the countenance of anyone other than Him. This belongs to the third degree: witnessing distinction within unified awareness, and multiplicity within oneness. Thus, he witnesses that all beings, despite their diversity, subsist solely through Allah Most High sustaining them and exercising His lordship over them. He recognizes that there is no creature but that my Lord holds it by its forelock; that He is the Disposer of all things and the Lord of the worlds; and that the servants’ hearts and forelocks are in His hand. There is no creator besides Him, nor anyone who brings benefit or harm, gives or withholds, preserves, honors or humiliates, other than Him. He also witnesses

---

## Page 842

the performance of commanded acts, despite their multitude, and the avoidance of doubtful matters, despite their multitude, for Allah alone, without any partner. This is the comprehensive, universal religion shared by all the prophets: Islam in its universal sense and faith in its universal sense. This is what the Meccan surahs were revealed with, and it is indicated by the words of Allah Most High:

“He has ordained for you the religion He enjoined upon Noah, and that which We have revealed to you, and that which We enjoined upon Abraham, Moses, and Jesus: uphold the religion and do not become divided in it.”

And by His words:

“Ask those of Our messengers whom We sent before you: did We appoint any gods besides the Most Merciful to be worshipped?”

And by the words of Allah Most High:

“We certainly sent a messenger to every nation, saying: worship Allah and shun false gods.”

For this reason, al-Bukhārī devoted a chapter to this, entitled “Reports Establishing That the Religion of the Prophets Is One.”

Allah Most High also said:

“Indeed, those who believed, the Jews, the Christians, and the Sabians—whoever believed in Allah and the Last Day and did righteous deeds—will have their reward with their Lord. No fear will be upon them, nor will they grieve.”

Thus, He brought together those within the four religious communities who:

“believed in Allah and the Last Day and did righteous deeds.”

This was before abrogation and alteration. At the beginning of the verse, He singled out the believers. This refers to faith in its specific, divinely prescribed sense, concerning which He said:

“For each of you We have prescribed a law and a way.”

The “law” is the Sharia, and the “way” is the path. The comprehensive religion is the religious reality, while affirming Allah’s oneness in His lordship is the cosmic reality. Thus, the religious reality that is sought and the cosmic reality that exists are matters of agreement among the prophets and messengers.

---

## Page 843

As for the Islamic law and way, these belong to the community of Muhammad, may Allah Most High bless him and grant him peace:

“The best nation brought forth for mankind.”

The Medinan surahs were revealed with this law and way, for it was in the Prophet’s city that laws were prescribed, practices established, and legal rulings, obligations, and prescribed penalties revealed.

This affirmation of Allah’s oneness is what the messengers brought and the scriptures were revealed with, and it is to this that the masters of the spiritual path and the scholars of religion point. Yet some who experience spiritual states may, in a deficient state of self-effacement, undergo intoxication and lose awareness of what is other. Intoxication is ecstasy without discernment.

In that state, a person may say, “Glory be to me,” or, “There is nothing in this robe but Allah,” or similar utterances attributed to Abū Yazīd al-Bistāmī or others who were otherwise sound. The words of an intoxicated person are to be put aside, neither narrated nor passed on, provided his intoxication was not caused by something forbidden, whether an act of worship or some prohibited means. If its cause was forbidden, however, the intoxicated person is not excused. In this respect, there is no difference between bodily and spiritual intoxication: bodies become intoxicated through food and drink, souls through forms, and spirits through sounds.

It was in such a state that some fell into error by claiming union and actual indwelling, like the Christians’ claims concerning Christ, the extremists’ claims concerning ʿAlī and the Prophet’s household, and the claims of certain ignorant extremists concerning such figures as al-Hallāj, al-Hākim in Egypt, or others. They may have confused unity in kind and judgment with actual identity of essence.

---

## Page 844

The former is illustrated by what Muslim narrated in his Sahīh from Abū Hurayrah, from the Prophet, may Allah Most High bless him and grant him peace, who said:

“Allah says, ‘My servant, I fell ill, yet you did not visit Me.’ He replies, ‘How could I visit You when You are the Lord of all worlds?’ He says, ‘Did you not know that My servant so-and-so fell ill? Had you visited him, you would have found Me with him. My servant, I was hungry, yet you did not feed Me.’ He replies, ‘My Lord, how could I feed You when You are the Lord of all worlds?’ He says, ‘Did you not know that My servant so-and-so was hungry? Had you fed him, you would have found that with Me.’”

Thus, He explained that His words in this hadith referred to the hunger of His servant, whom He loves, by saying, “You would have found that with Me.” He did not say, “You would have found that I had eaten it.” Likewise, He said, “You would have found Me with him,” not, “You would have found Me to be him.” This is because the lover and the beloved are in accord: one is pleased with what pleases the other, commands what the other commands, hates what the other hates, dislikes what the other dislikes, and forbids what the other forbids. These are the ones for whose pleasure Allah is pleased, and for whose anger He is angry. The one who is perfect in every respect among them is Muhammad, may Allah Most High bless him and grant him peace.

For this reason, Allah Most High said concerning him:

“Indeed, those who pledge allegiance to you are actually pledging allegiance to Allah.”

And He said:

“But Allah and His Messenger are more worthy for them to please Him.”

And He said:

“Whoever obeys the Messenger has obeyed Allah.”

“In the Gospel in the Christians’ possession, there are statements open to interpretation. If it is true that Christ said them, this is their meaning—as in his saying, ‘I and my Father are one. Whoever has seen me has seen my Father’”—and similar statements

---

## Page 845

and through these statements the Christians went astray, following what was ambiguous, as Allah related concerning them in the Quran when the delegation from Najrān came to the Prophet, may Allah Most High bless him and grant him peace, and debated with him concerning Christ.

In the authentic hadith narrated by al-Bukhārī from Abū Hurayrah, the Messenger of Allah, may Allah Most High bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Whoever shows hostility to a friend of Mine has openly challenged Me to war. Nothing brings My servant nearer to Me like fulfilling what I have made obligatory upon him. My servant continues to draw near to Me through voluntary acts of worship until I love him. When I love him, I become his hearing with which he hears, his sight with which he sees, his hand with which he grasps, and his foot with which he walks. Through Me he hears, through Me he sees, through Me he grasps, and through Me he walks.”

In this hadith, he explained that when the servant draws near to the True One, glory be to Him, through the recommended voluntary acts that Allah loves, after fulfilling the obligatory acts, the True One loves him in this manner. Those who claim that this is closeness through voluntary acts, whereas closeness through obligatory acts means that the two are one and the same, are mistaken. Allah does not accept a voluntary act until the obligatory act has been performed. Thus, this closeness encompasses both obligatory and voluntary acts. These meanings, and others like them, are the foundations of the teaching of those who follow the Islamic spiritual path—the followers of the prophets and messengers.

I have learned that some people have discussed the doctrine of the advocates of union with God in your presence. I had previously written you a letter in which circumstances led me, without prior intention, to make a subtle allusion to the condition of these people. By Allah, I did not intend any particular individual by it. Rather, the shaykh is a rallying point for the believers, and we must assist him in religious and worldly affairs in a manner that befits him. As for these advocates of union, someone has sent word to this supplicant requesting that the true nature of their position be disclosed.

---

## Page 846

And I have written a treatise on this matter which may be sent to the shaykh. Our master, Shaykh ‘Imād al-Dīn, has also written letters on the subject. Allah Most High knows—and He suffices as Knower—that, were it not that I regard protecting those who follow the path of Allah Most High and journey towards Him from the harm of these people as one of the greatest obligations—comparable to defending the believers against the Tatars—those who believe in Allah and His Messenger would have no need for the secrets of the path to be disclosed or its veils torn away. Yet the shaykh—may Allah Most High be gracious to him—knows that the purpose of the prophetic call, indeed, the purpose of creating all creatures, sending down the Scriptures, and sending the messengers, is that all religion should be devoted to Allah alone. It is to call creatures to their Creator, as He Most High says:

“Indeed, We have sent you as a witness, a bearer of good tidings, and a warner, and as one who calls to Allah by His permission, and as an illuminating lamp.”

And He, glory be to Him, says:

“Say, ‘This is my way: I call to Allah with clear insight—I and those who follow me.’”

And He Most High says:

“And indeed, you guide to a straight path—the path of Allah, to whom belongs whatever is in the heavens and whatever is on earth. Surely, to Allah all matters return.”

These people have misled those on the spiritual path by confusing monotheism—for which Allah Most High sent down the Scriptures and sent the messengers—with union with God, which they have called monotheism, though its true nature is the negation of the Maker and the denial of the Creator.

In earlier times, I was among those who thought well of Ibn ‘Arabī and held him in high esteem because of the beneficial insights I found in his books, such as his discussions in much of “al-Futūhāt,” and in “al-Kinnah,” “al-Muhkam al-Marbūt,” “al-Durrah al-Fākhirah,” “Matāli‘ al-Nujūm,” and similar works. We had not yet become acquainted with

---

## Page 847

the true nature of his intent, nor had we read al-Fusūs or similar works. We would gather with our brothers in Allah, seeking and following the truth and uncovering the true nature of the spiritual path. Once the matter became clear, we understood our duty.

When respected shaykhs arrived from the East and asked about the true nature of the Islamic path and the Islamic religion, and about the actual condition of these people, clarification became obligatory. Likewise, sincere men following the spiritual path wrote to us from the outlying regions of Syria, and I was asked to set out the key points that collectively explain the true nature of their intent. The shaykh—may Allah Most High strengthen him through the light of his heart and the acuity of his soul, and fulfill his purpose in his sincere concern for Islam, its people, and his brothers on the spiritual path—acts in this matter hoping to attain Allah’s pleasure and forgiveness in this world and the Hereafter.

As for these people who have spoken on this matter, no account of them has been known since the rise of the Tatar state. Otherwise, the earlier doctrine of union was union confined to a particular individual. The classification is fourfold, since both union and indwelling are either confined to a particular individual or unrestricted.

Union and indwelling confined to a particular individual are exemplified by the beliefs of the Christians, the extremists among the Rāfidah concerning their imams, and the ignorant spiritual devotees and Sufis concerning their shaykhs. They maintain these beliefs concerning a particular individual: either union, like the mingling of water and milk, which is the doctrine of the Jacobites, who comprise black peoples and people from among the Abyssinians and Copts; or indwelling, which is the doctrine of the Nestorians; or union in one respect but not another, which is the doctrine of the Melkites.

---

## Page 848

As for unrestricted indwelling—the belief that Allah Most High Himself dwells in everything—the adherents of the Sunnah and the early generations attribute this belief to the early Jahmiyyah, whom they would declare unbelievers on that account. As for the universal union introduced by these people, I know of no one who preceded them in it except those who denied the existence of the Creator, such as Pharaoh and the Qarmatians.

The reality of their position is that they regard the very existence of the Real as the very existence of creation, and hold that the existence of the essence of Allah, the Creator of the heavens and the earth, is precisely the existence of created things. Thus, in their view, it is inconceivable that Allah Most High created anything other than Himself, or that He is the Lord of the worlds, or that He is self-sufficient while everything besides Him is in need. Yet they have divided into three approaches. Most people who examine their words do not understand the reality of their position, for it is an obscure matter.

(The first)

They say that all entities were established in nonexistence, their essences being eternal, without beginning or end—even the essences of animals, plants, minerals, motions, and states of rest. They hold that the existence of the Real flowed forth upon those entities: their existence is therefore the existence of the Real, but their essences are not those of the Real. They distinguish between existence and being established: whatever you were in your state of being established is what you manifest in your existence.

They say:

“Allah, glorified be He, has given no one anything, made no one free of need, and made no one happy or wretched. Rather, His existence flowed forth upon the entities. So praise only yourself, and blame only yourself.”

---

## Page 849

They also say that this is the secret of divine decree, and that Allah Most High knew things only by seeing them established in nonexistence, outside His sacred Self.

They say that Allah Most High cannot change a single particle in the world, and that they may know things from the same source through which Allah, glorified be He, knew them. Their knowledge and Allah’s knowledge would thus come from one source. They also say that they are superior to the Seal of the Messengers in certain respects, because they draw from the source from which the angel through whom the messengers receive revelation was drawn.

They say that they have never worshipped anyone other than Allah, that it is inconceivable for them to worship anyone other than Allah Most High, and that idol worshippers have worshipped none but Allah, glorified be He. They claim that His words:

“Your Lord has decreed that you worship none but Him”

mean that He determined this, not that He commanded it. Thus, in every object of worship, none but Allah has been worshipped, for whatever Allah Most High decrees necessarily comes to pass.

They say that calling people to Allah Most High is a deception practised upon those being called, for He was not absent at the outset, such that one should be called to Him at the end. They say that Noah’s people declared:

“Never abandon your gods, and never abandon Wadd or Suwā'”

because, had they abandoned them, they would have abandoned the Real to the same extent. For the Real has an aspect in every object of worship, recognized by those who recognize it and denied by those who deny it. They say that differentiation and multiplicity are like the limbs of a physical form and the immaterial faculties of a spiritual form, and that the gnostic among them knows whom he has worshipped and in what form He appeared to be worshipped.

For the ignorant person says, “This is stone and tree,” whereas the gnostic says, “This is a locus of divine manifestation that ought to be revered.” He therefore does not confine himself, for the Christians became unbelievers only because they singled out

---

## Page 850

and that idol worshippers erred only by confining their worship to certain manifestations, whereas the gnostic worships everything. Allah, too, worships everything, for things are His nourishment in respect of names and determinations, while He is their nourishment in respect of existence. He needs them, and they need Him; in this sense, He is the intimate friend of everything. They make Allah’s most beautiful names mere relations and connections between existence and established reality, and not nonexistent entities.

They say:

“Among His most beautiful names is the Most High. Exalted beyond what, when there is nothing but Him? And above what, when there is nothing other than Him? The name therefore designates originated things, which are exalted in themselves and are none other than He. He has had intercourse with none but Himself and has slaughtered none but Himself; the speaker is the very same as the listener.”

They also say that Moses rebuked Aaron only for forbidding them to worship the calf, because of Aaron’s limited understanding and lack of breadth, whereas Moses had broader knowledge and thus knew that they had worshipped none but Allah. They say that the highest object of worship is desire, and that whoever takes his own desire as his god has worshipped none but Allah. In their view, Pharaoh was one of the greatest gnostics, and the magicians affirmed the truth of his words:

“I am your lord, the most high,”

and his words:

“I know of no god for you other than myself.”

I used to expose their position in discussions with some learned men who had gone astray, saying that the reality of their position was precisely that of Pharaoh, who denied the existence of the Creator and Maker, until someone reported to me from many of their leading figures that they acknowledge this and say, “We adhere to Pharaoh’s position.”

---

## Page 851

All these ideas are the teachings of the author of al-Fusūs. Allah Most High knows best what beliefs the man held when he died. May Allah forgive all Muslim men and women and all believing men and women, both living and dead.

“Our Lord, forgive us and our brothers who preceded us in faith, and put no resentment in our hearts towards those who believe. Our Lord, indeed You are Kind and Merciful.”

The point is that the actual content of al-Fusūs—which is attributed to the Prophet, may Allah Most High bless him and grant him peace, with the claim that he brought it—is such that, once a Muslim understands it, he knows with absolute certainty that all the prophets and messengers, all the friends of Allah and the righteous, and indeed even all ordinary adherents of the religious communities—Jews, Christians, and Sabians—disavow before Allah Most High even part of this doctrine. How much more, then, its entirety!

We know that idol-worshipping polytheists and unbelievers among the People of the Book acknowledge the existence of the Maker, the Creator, the Originator, the Fashioner, who created the heavens and the earth and brought darkness and light into being—their Lord and the Lord of their forefathers, the Lord of the East and the West. None of them says that He is identical with created beings or is Himself the things He has made, as these people say. They even say that if the heavens and the earth ceased to exist, the reality of Allah would cease to exist.

This doctrine is composed of two premises:

The first is that a nonexistent thing is an entity established in nonexistence, as many of the Muʿtazilah and the Rāfidah maintain. This doctrine is refuted by reason that accords with the Book, the Sunnah, and consensus. Many speculative theologians who affirm the divine attributes—such as al-Qādī Abū Bakr—have declared anyone who holds this view an unbeliever.

---

## Page 852

These people erred only because they failed to distinguish between Allah’s knowledge of things before they come into being—and their being recorded with Him in the Mother of the Book, the Preserved Tablet—and their being established in external reality, distinct from Allah Most High’s knowledge. The belief of the Muslims of Ahl al-Sunnah wa-l-Jamāʿah is that Allah, glorified and exalted is He, recorded the decrees of created beings in the Preserved Tablet before creating them. They therefore distinguish between existence in knowledge and concrete existence in external reality.

For this reason, the first revelation to the Messenger of Allah, may Allah Most High bless him and grant him peace, was the surah:

“Read in the name of your Lord who created,”
“Created man from a clinging clot.”
“Read, and your Lord is the Most Generous,”
“Who taught by the pen,”
“Taught man what he did not know.”

He thus mentioned the four modes of existence: concrete existence, which He created, and existence in writing, which corresponds to verbal existence and indicates existence in knowledge. He also made clear that Allah Most High knew it. This is why He mentioned teaching by the pen, for it necessarily entails the three modes.

This doctrine—I mean the claim that a nonexistent thing is an entity established in itself, independently of Allah Most High’s knowledge—although false and clear in its implications, was nevertheless introduced into Islam some four hundred years ago. Ibn ʿArabī agreed with its proponents, and it is one of the two foundations of his doctrine in al-Fusūs.

The second foundation is that the existence of things brought into being and created is identical with the existence of the Creator: it is neither different from it nor anything other than it. This is what he innovated, setting himself apart from all the spiritual masters and scholars who preceded him. It is also the doctrine of the other advocates of the identity of Creator and creation. Ibn ʿArabī, however, is the closest of them to Islam, and his statements are better in many places, for he distinguishes between that which manifests and the forms in which it manifests

---

## Page 853

and so he upholds commands, prohibitions, and religious laws as they stand, and enjoins adherence to many of the moral virtues and acts of worship prescribed by the spiritual masters. For this reason, many worshippers derive guidance for their spiritual conduct from his words and benefit from it, even though they do not understand his underlying doctrines. Whoever among them understands those doctrines and agrees with him has indeed understood his position.

(As for) his companion al-Sadr al-Rūmī, he was given to philosophizing and was therefore further removed from religious law and Islam. Hence the wicked al-Tilmisānī, known as al-ʿAfīf (the Chaste), used to say: “My former master was a mystic with philosophical leanings, whereas the other was a philosopher with mystical leanings”—meaning al-Sadr al-Rūmī. He had studied under him but had not met Ibn ʿArabī. In Miftāh Ghayb al-Jamʿ wa-l-Wujūd and other works, al-Rūmī says that Allah Most High is absolute and particular existence, just as a distinction is drawn between an animal considered in the absolute and a particular animal, and between a body considered in the absolute and a particular body. The absolute exists only in external reality as something absolute; the absolute exists only in concrete external entities.

His position therefore amounts to saying that Allah, glorified is He, has no existence whatsoever, no reality, and no established being other than the very existence subsisting in created beings. For this reason, he and his master say that Allah Most High does not see at all and that, in reality, He has neither name nor attribute. They explicitly declare that the very entities of the dog, the pig, urine, and excrement are identical with His existence—exalted is Allah above what they say.

(As for) the wicked al-Tilmisānī, he is the most vile of these people and the most deeply immersed in disbelief. He does not distinguish between existence and established being as Ibn ʿArabī does, nor does he distinguish between the absolute and the particular

---

## Page 854

as al-Rūmī does. Rather, in his view, there is no other and nothing distinct in any respect whatsoever. He holds that the servant perceives something other only while veiled; once his veil is lifted, he sees that no other exists, and the matter becomes clear to him.

For this reason, he deemed all forbidden things lawful. Trustworthy people even reported that he used to say: “A daughter, a mother, and an unrelated woman are all one and the same. None of this is forbidden to us. It was only these veiled people who said, ‘It is forbidden,’ so we said, ‘It is forbidden to you.’” He also used to say: “The Quran is entirely polytheism; it contains no affirmation of divine oneness. Divine oneness is affirmed only in our words.”

He would say: “I do not adhere to a single religious law.” When he phrased it more favorably, he would say: “The Quran leads to Paradise, whereas our words lead to Allah Most High.” He expounded the Most Beautiful Names according to this principle of his. He also has a collection of poetry containing various compositions, and his verse displays fine poetic craftsmanship. Yet, as the saying goes, it is “pork on a porcelain platter.” He also composed a creed for the Nusayrīs. Their doctrine, in essence, is that the Real is like the sea, and the constituent parts of existing things are like its waves.

As for Ibn Sabʿīn, in al-Badw wa-l-Ihāta he likewise espouses the unity of existence and holds that no other exists. Ibn al-Fārid does the same at the end of Nazm al-Sulūk (The Poem of Spiritual Wayfaring), although he does not explicitly state whether his position resembles that of al-Tilmisānī, al-Rūmī, or Ibn ʿArabī. His words are closer to those of al-Tilmisānī. Yet I have not seen anyone among them fall into this disbelief, which

---

## Page 855

no one has ever committed to such an extent as al-Tilmisānī and another man called al-Balyānī, one of the shaykhs of Shiraz.

Among his verses are:

In everything there is a sign of Him,
showing that He is that very thing.

And also:

You are not other than the universe; rather, you are its very self.
This secret is understood by one who has tasted it.

And also:

My hand delights if it passes over my body,
for in truth I am none other than you.

And also:

Why do your white camels never come to rest?
How long will your shadow keep moving on?
You will surely learn, when you reach your destination,
that your journey was only ever toward yourself.

And also:

All is but one single order,
with neither praise nor blame within it.
Only custom, nature, and the Lawgiver
have made distinctions in judgment.

And also:

O you who reproach me, you forbid me and command me,
but ecstasy is truer in its prohibitions and commands.
If I obey you and disobey ecstasy, I would turn back, blind,
from direct vision to the illusions of hearsay.

---

## Page 856

For if you examine what you call me to,
you will find it is the very thing forbidden, O my neighbor.

And also:

The sea is nothing but waves, nothing else,
even if multiplicity divides it.

There are other poems of this kind, and countless examples in prose. They deceive the ignorant into thinking that they are the shaykhs of Islam and leaders of right guidance whom Allah Most High has granted an honorable reputation within the Muslim community—such as Sa‘īd ibn al-Musayyib, al-Hasan al-Basrī, ‘Umar ibn ‘Abd al-‘Azīz, Mālik ibn Anas, al-Awzā‘ī, Ibrāhīm ibn Adham, Sufyān al-Thawrī, al-Fudayl ibn ‘Iyād, Ma‘rūf al-Karkhī, al-Shāfi‘ī, Abū Sulaymān, Ahmad ibn Hanbal, Bishr al-Hāfī, ‘Abd Allāh ibn al-Mubārak, Shaqīq al-Balkhī, and countless others.

These include later figures such as al-Junayd ibn Muhammad al-Qawārīrī, Sahl ibn ‘Abd Allāh al-Tustarī, ‘Umar ibn ‘Uthmān al-Makkī, and those who came after them—down to Abū Tālib al-Makkī, and then figures such as Shaykh ‘Abd al-Qādir al-Kīlānī, Shaykh ‘Adī, Shaykh Abū al-Bayān, Shaykh Abū Madyan, Shaykh ‘Aqīl, Shaykh Abū al-Wafā’, Shaykh Raslān, Shaykh ‘Abd al-Rahīm, Shaykh ‘Abd Allāh al-Yūnīnī, Shaykh al-Qurashī, and other shaykhs like them who lived in the Hijaz, Syria, Iraq, Egypt, the Maghrib, and Khurasan, among both the earlier and later generations. All of them agree that these people, and those superior to them, are unbelievers, and that Allah—glorified is He—

---

## Page 857

is neither His creation, nor a part of His creation, nor an attribute of His creation. Rather, He—glorified and exalted is He—is distinct in His holy self and separate, in His exalted essence, from His creatures. This is what the four divine scriptures—the Torah, the Gospel, the Psalms, and the Quran—teach. Allah Most High has endowed His servants with an innate disposition to recognize this, and reason likewise points to it.

I have often thought that the emergence of people like these is the foremost cause of the rise of the Tatars and the effacement of Islamic law, and that these people are the forerunners of the one-eyed, lying Dajjāl, who claims that he is Allah. For in their view, everything is Allah, but some things are greater and mightier than others.

According to the author of al-Fusūs, some loci of manifestation and self-disclosure are greater because of the greatness of their essences, which are fixed in nonexistence. According to al-Rūmī, some individual entities are greater, since some particular instances of a universal are greater than others. According to the rest, all things are parts of Him, and some parts are greater than others.

Thus, in their view, the Dajjāl, like Pharaoh, is among the great knowers of Allah and is greater than the messengers after our Prophet Muhammad—may Allah Most High bless him and grant him peace—and Abraham, Moses, and Jesus—peace be upon them. Moses is the slayer of Pharaoh, who claims lordship; and Allah Most High will give the Messiah of guidance—of whom it was said that he is Allah Most High, though he is innocent of this—power over the messiah of misguidance, who said that he is Allah.

---

## Page 858

For this reason, some people were puzzled that the Prophet—may Allah Most High bless him and grant him peace—said:

“He is one-eyed,”

and that he said:

“Know that none of you will see his Lord until he dies.”

Ibn al-Khatīb denied that the Prophet—may Allah Most High bless him and grant him peace—had said this, because the signs that the Dajjāl had a beginning and was imperfect were too obvious to require refuting his claim by pointing out that he was one-eyed.

But when we understood the true nature of the teachings of these proponents of union and reflected on the errors into which the Christians and proponents of divine indwelling had fallen, the reason why the Prophet—may Allah Most High bless him and grant him peace—directed his community to this sign became clear. He was sent as a mercy to all worlds. Since many people consider it possible for the Lord to manifest Himself in human beings, or maintain that He is Himself those human beings, pointing to the Dajjāl’s blindness in one eye provides evidence that he is not divine.

Long ago, one of the finest of our associates—who had inclined toward the doctrine of union but later repented of it—spoke to me and mentioned this hadith, and I explained its significance to him.

A man also came to us who claimed to be the Seal of the Saints. He asserted that when al-Hallāj said, “I am the Truth,” it was Allah Most High speaking through his tongue, just as a jinn speaks through the tongue of a possessed person. He further claimed that when the Companions heard the speech of Allah Most High from the Prophet—may Allah Most High bless him and grant him peace—it was of this same kind.

I explained to him the falsity of this claim: if that were so, the Companions would have had the same standing as Moses son of ʿImrān, and those whom these people addressed would have been greater than Moses. For Moses heard the divine speech from the tree, whereas these people hear it from the speaking jinn.

---

## Page 859

And some proponents of union say this. Most of them, however, are ignorant: they do not distinguish between the universal, absolute union espoused by the wicked al-Tilimsānī and his like, and union with particular beings, as espoused by the Christians and the extremists.

The early generations of the Muslim community and its foremost imams considered the unbelief of the Jahmiyyah greater than that of the Jews, as stated by ‘Abd Allah ibn al-Mubārak, al-Bukhārī, and others. This position was generally only hinted at; seldom was it stated explicitly that His essence was in a place. These proponents of union, however, are more vile and more deeply steeped in unbelief than those Jahmiyyah. Yet the early generations and the imams understood Islam and its realities better. Many people may not understand the severity of their condemnation of this doctrine until they reflect upon it and are granted the light of guidance. When the early generations discerned what lay beneath this teaching, they recoiled from it.

This accords with the saying:

“The speculative theologians of the Jahmiyyah worship nothing, while their devotees worship everything.”

This is because their speculative theologian has neither devotion nor worship in his heart, and so describes his Lord with the attributes of nonexistence and lifelessness. Their devotee, however, has devotion and worship in his heart. Since the heart turns only toward something existent, not something nonexistent, he finds himself needing to worship created things—either absolute existence or particular manifestations, such as the sun, the moon, human beings, idols, and other things.

The doctrine of these proponents of union encompasses every form of associating partners with Allah found in the world. They do not affirm the oneness of Allah—glorified and exalted is He—but only the oneness of what He and created beings have in common. Thus they set others equal to their Lord.

---

## Page 860

For this reason, a trustworthy man told me that Ibn Sab‘īn wished to go to India and said that the lands of Islam had no room for him; for the people of India are polytheists who worship everything, even plants and animals. This is the true substance of the doctrine of the proponents of union.

I know people engaged in philosophy and speculative theology who have adopted the devotional ways of these proponents of union. When they begin describing the Lord—glorified is He—through speculative theology, they say, “He is not this; He is not that.” They describe Him as not being the Lord of created beings in the sense affirmed by Muslims. Instead, they deny the attributes of the Creator taught by the messengers, peace be upon them.

But when one of them acquires spiritual experience and feeling, he turns to devotion, follows the path of the proponents of union, and says, “He is all existing things.” When asked, “How can that negation be reconciled with this affirmation?” he replies, “That is my spiritual feeling, and this is my spiritual experience.”

This misguided person should be told: Whenever spiritual experience or feeling conflicts with belief, one or both must be false. Spiritual experiences and feelings are nothing other than the products of understanding and beliefs. The heart’s knowledge and its state are inseparably linked: its spiritual feeling, love, and state correspond to the extent of its knowledge and understanding.

Had these people followed the path of the prophets and messengers, peace be upon them—who commanded the worship of Allah Most High alone, without any partner, and described Him as He described Himself and as His messengers described Him—and followed the path of the earliest forerunners, they would have followed the path of guidance and found the soothing comfort of certainty and the delight of their hearts. For it is as someone said: “The messengers

---

## Page 861

came with detailed affirmation and general negation, whereas the Sabians who deny the divine attributes came with detailed negation and general affirmation.” The Quran abounds in statements of affirmation, such as His saying, Most High:

“Indeed, Allah has knowledge of all things.”

It also affirms that He has power over all things, that He is All-Hearing, All-Seeing, and that He encompasses all things in mercy and knowledge. As for negation:

“There is nothing like Him.”

“And there is none comparable to Him.”

“Do you know of any equal to Him?”

“Glory be to your Lord, the Lord of Might, above what they attribute to Him.”

“And peace be upon the messengers.”

Although I have addressed the shaykh at length in this letter—may Allah Most High strengthen Islam through him and benefit the Muslims through the blessing of his breaths, his good intentions, and the light of his heart—it contains only brief points. These matters cannot be fully explained in a letter. Rather, I have mentioned to the shaykh—may Allah Most High be gracious to him—what the circumstances required me to mention. The bearer of this letter is mustawfī and in a hurry.

I ask Allah the Almighty to set right the affairs of the Muslims, both their common people and their leading figures, to guide them to what brings them nearer to Him, and to make the shaykh one of those who call to goodness, concerning whom Allah, glorified is He, said:

“Let there be among you a group calling to goodness, enjoining what is right and forbidding what is wrong. It is they who are successful.”

---

## Page 862

Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah sanctify his soul—was asked:

“What do the leading scholars of Islam say about al-Hallāj? What ruling applies to someone who says, ‘I believe what al-Hallāj believed,’ and says, ‘He was killed unjustly, just as some of the prophets were killed,’ and says, ‘Al-Hallāj is one of Allah’s righteous friends’? What ruling applies to him for saying this, and was al-Hallāj executed in accordance with the Sharia?”

He answered:

Praise be to Allah. Whoever holds the beliefs for which al-Hallāj was executed is a disbeliever and an apostate, by the unanimous agreement of the Muslims. The Muslims executed him for his doctrines of divine indwelling and union with God, and similar teachings of those given to heresy and unbelief, such as his saying, “I am Allah,” and his saying, “A god in heaven and a god on earth.”

It is necessarily known in the religion of Islam that there is no god but Allah, that Allah is the Creator of all things, and that everything besides Him is created, and:

“There is no one in the heavens and earth but that he comes to the Most Merciful as a servant.”

He said, Most High:

“O People of the Scripture, do not commit excess in your religion or say about Allah except the truth,”

and the subsequent verses. He also said, Most High:

“They have certainly disbelieved who say, ‘Allah is the Messiah, the son of Mary,’”

and the following verse. Thus the Christians whom Allah and His Messenger declared to be disbelievers, and whom the Muslims unanimously regard as disbelieving in Allah

---

## Page 863

and His Messenger, held among their foremost doctrines that God dwelt in and united with the Messiah, son of Mary. Whoever professes divine indwelling and union with anyone other than the Messiah—as the extremists claim concerning ʿAlī, the followers of al-Hallāj concerning al-Hallāj, and the followers of al-Hākim concerning al-Hākim, and others like them—holds a doctrine worse than that of the Christians, for the Messiah, son of Mary, is superior to all these figures.

These people are of the same kind as the followers of the Dajjāl (the False Messiah), who claims divinity so that people will follow him. Yet the Dajjāl says to the sky, “Send down rain,” and it rains; to the earth, “Bring forth vegetation,” and it does so; and to the ruins, “Bring forth your treasures,” and their treasures of gold and silver come out with him. He kills a believing man, then gives the command, and the man rises. Despite all this, he is the one-eyed liar, the Dajjāl. Whoever claims divinity without such extraordinary feats is even lesser than this Dajjāl.

Al-Hallāj performed tricks and practised various forms of sorcery, and books on sorcery are attributed to him.

In short, there is no disagreement within the Muslim community that anyone who professes that Allah dwells in a human being and unites with him, that a human being can become a god, and that this person is one of the gods, is a disbeliever whose blood may lawfully be shed. It was on this basis that al-Hallāj was executed.

Whoever says that Allah spoke through al-Hallāj’s tongue, that the speech heard from al-Hallāj was Allah’s speech, and that it was Allah who said through his tongue, “I am Allah,” is a disbeliever by the unanimous agreement of the Muslims. For Allah does not dwell in human beings, nor has He spoken through a human tongue. Rather, He sends messengers with His speech, and they convey on His behalf what He has commanded them to communicate. Thus, through the tongues of the messengers, He says what He has commanded them

---

## Page 864

to say, as the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Indeed, Allah has said through the tongue of His Prophet, ‘Allah hears whoever praises Him.’”

Both the sender and the messenger may be said to speak through the other’s tongue. Thus, Imam Ahmad ibn Hanbal said to al-Marwadhī, “Say whatever you wish on my behalf.” Likewise, one may say, “This man says such-and-such on the ruler’s behalf.” The meaning of such expressions is understood.

But to claim that Allah Himself speaks through a human being, as a jinn speaks through the tongue of a possessed person, is outright disbelief. If, however, such words come from someone who has lost his reason and is exempt from accountability because he is utterly overwhelmed in a state of self-effacement and spiritual intoxication, then he has spoken while exempt from accountability in those states. Although the statement is false, its speaker is not held accountable.

Something like this may happen to someone whose reason is weak and who is overpowered by love. Thus, it is related that a beloved threw himself into the sea, and his lover threw himself in after him. The beloved said, “I fell in; why did you follow me in?” He replied, “So absorbed was I in you that I lost awareness of myself and thought that you were me.”
